{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]\leq\frac{\min_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}\right)}{\min_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P}^{e},\mathcal{G}^{e}\right)}.

If, also,
\Psi_{1,1}\subseteq\Psi
, and there is a pair of data sources that is independent of all other data sources under
{\sf P}
and achieves

\min_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\;\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{e},\mathcal{G}^{e})
, e.g., when (
23
) holds, then
{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]=1.

The proof can be found in Appendix
G.6
.
∎

Finally, we consider the case that it is a priori known that all signals are disjoint pairs, i.e.,
\Psi=\Psi_{dis}
, and we establish the asymptotic optimality of the

Intersection rule
, defined in (
6
), whenever (
23
) holds and one of the most difficult to isolate, independent pairs is independent of all other data sources.

Theorem 6.6
.

Suppose that
\Psi=\Psi_{dis}
,
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}
and
s_{e}=s^{\prime}_{e}=e
for every
e\in\mathcal{K}
. If (
23
) holds, and there is a pair of data sources that is independent of all other data sources under
{\sf P}
and achieves
\min_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\;\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{e},\mathcal{G}^{e})
, then

\chi^{*}_{fwer}
is asymptotically optimal as

\gamma,\delta\to 0
.

The proof can be found in Appendix
G.7
.
∎

6.2
Detection and isolation of a Gaussian dependence structure

We next specialize the results of this section to the setup of Subsection
2.4.2
, when it is assumed a priori that a subset of data sources follow an equicorrelated multivariate Gaussian distribution with common positive correlation
\rho
, and are independent of the rest of the sources. Specifically, we show that when there is only one such subset, of size
m
, the other sources are independent, and
\gamma
(resp.
\delta
) goes to
0
faster than
\alpha
and
\delta
(resp.
\gamma
), then the asymptotic relative efficiency of
\chi^{*}
, defined as in (
22
), with
s_{e}=s^{\prime}_{e}=e
for every
e\in\mathcal{K}
,
is bounded above by a quantity that depends only on
\rho
and
m
. This upper bound approaches 1 as
\rho
increases, while
m
is fixed, and some number strictly greater than 1 as
m
increases, while
\rho
is fixed. We illustrate this upper bound in Figure
1
.

Corollary 6.1
.

Consider the setup of Subsection
2.4.2
with
\mathcal{R}_{ij}=\{0,\rho\}
for every
i,j\in[K]\;\text{and}\;i<j
. Suppose that
\Psi=\Psi_{clus}
and
s_{e}=s^{\prime}_{e}=e
for every
e\in\mathcal{K}
. Let
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}
such that
L({\sf P})=1
and
|v_{1}|=m
.

(i)

If
2<m\leq K
and
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\to 0
such that
|\log\gamma|>>|\log\alpha|\vee|\log\delta|
, then

\displaystyle{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]

\displaystyle\leq\log\left(1-\rho\frac{\rho(m-1)}{1+\rho(m-2)}\right)/\log(1-\rho^{2})\leq\frac{\log(1-\rho)}{\log(1-\rho^{2})}.

(ii)

If
2\leq m<K
and
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\to 0
such that
|\log\delta|>>|\log\alpha|\vee|\log\gamma|
, then

\displaystyle{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]

\displaystyle\leq\left\{\log\sqrt{1-\frac{\rho^{2}m}{1+\rho(m-1)}}+\frac{\rho^{2}m}{(1+\rho m)(1-\rho)}\right\}\bigg/\left\{\log\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}+\frac{\rho^{2}}{1-\rho^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\log(1-\rho)+\frac{2\rho}{1-\rho}\right)\bigg/\left(\log(1-\rho^{2})+\frac{2\rho^{2}}{1-\rho^{2}}\right).

(iii)

If
2\leq m\leq K
and
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\to 0
such that
|\log\alpha|>>|\log\gamma|\vee|\log\delta|
, then

{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]\leq\frac{(m-1)\log(1-\rho)+\log(1+(m-1)\rho)}{\log(1-\rho^{2})}.

The proof can be found in Appendix
G.8
.
∎

Fig 1:
The graph in the left/right refers to the upper bounds of part (i)/(ii) of Corollary
6.1
(i). In both of them, the curves represent the bounds that depend on both
m
and
\rho
, whereas the straight lines the bounds that depend only on
\rho
.

7
Simulation Study

In this section we present the results of two simulation studies for the problem of joint detection and isolation of a dependence structure in Section
6
. In both of them
we consider the Gaussian setup of Subsection
2.4.2
, fix the marginal distributions of the sources to be standard Gaussian, i.e., we set
\mathcal{M}_{k}=\{0\}
,
\mathcal{V}_{k}=\{1\}
for every
k\in[K]
, and we consider a
two-sided
testing problem for each correlation coefficient, i.e., we set
\mathcal{R}_{ij}=\{0,\pm\rho\}
for each
i,j\in[K]
with
i<j
, where
\rho=0.7
.

In the first study, there are
K=7
data sources and two clusters,
\{4,5\}
and
\{1,2,3\}
. In particular,
\{4,5\}
is a dependent pair, with correlation
\rho
, independent of all other sources,
\{6,7\}
is an independent pair, also independent of all other sources, and the covariance matrix that corresponds to
\{1,2,3\}
is

\begin{bmatrix}1&\rho&-\rho\\
\rho&1&-\rho\\
-\rho&-\rho&1\end{bmatrix}.

(24)

In the second study there are
K=3
data sources, whose covariance matrix is (
24
).

In both studies we consider two versions of the testing procedure,
\chi^{*}
, in both of which the isolation and detection subsystems coincide, i.e.,
s_{e}=s^{\prime}_{e}
for every
e\in\mathcal{K}
. In the first version, the subsystems are of size 2, i.e.,
s_{e}=e
for every
e\in\mathcal{K}
. In the second, they are of size 3 (the third source in each subsystem, other than the pair itself, is chosen in some arbitrary way). For each of these two versions of
\chi^{*}
,
we consider two further cases depending on the available prior information. In the first one, there is no prior information whatsoever, i.e.,
\Psi
is equal to the powerset of
\mathcal{K}
, whereas in the second, it is known a priori that there is a dependence structure of cluster type, i.e.,
\Psi=\Psi_{clus}
.

For each of the resulting four versions of
\chi^{*}
and each value of the target error probabilities,
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta
, we select the thresholds according to Theorem
3.2
(iii).
When the subsystems are of size
2
, for the constants
\{a_{e},b_{e},c_{e},d_{e}\}_{e\in\mathcal{K}}
in (
7
) we have
a_{e}=c_{e}=1
,
b_{e}=d_{e}=2
for every
e\in\mathcal{K}
, regardless of
\Psi
and
K
. When the subsystems are of size
3
, we have
a_{e}=a
,
b_{e}=b
,
c_{e}=c
,
d_{e}=d

for every
e\in\mathcal{K}
, where
a,b,c,d
are specified in Table
1
. Moreover, we set

\alpha=\beta=\gamma=\delta
, i.e., all error metrics are of the same order of magnitude.

Table 1:

6

In the context of the first study, conditions (
15
) and (
16
) hold for all four versions of
\chi^{*}
,
since the conditions of Theorem
6.2
(ii) and Theorem
6.3
(ii) are satisfied. Furthermore, since
\alpha=\gamma
and the true distribution
{\sf P}
satisfies (see, in particular, Lemma
B.2
in Appendix
B
)

\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0})\geq\min_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{e},\mathcal{H}^{e})=\min_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}),

by Theorem
4.6
it follows that all four versions of
\chi^{*}
are asymptotically optimal as
\alpha\to 0
.

In the context of the second study, the versions of
\chi^{*}
, with subsystems of size 3 remain asymptotically optimal, as in this case we have
s_{e}=s^{\prime}_{e}=[K]
for every
e\in\mathcal{K}
. However, this is not true for the versions of
\chi^{*}
with subsystems of size 2, which use only local data to isolate the global dependence structure.

For each study, we vary
\alpha
from
10^{-8}
to
10^{-1}
, and estimate the expected sample size of each test using
16,000
Monte Carlo replications.
We observe that the simplest version of
\chi^{*}
performs the same regardless of the presence of prior information since the number of sources is small in both studies.
We plot the results for the first study in Figure
2
and for the second study in Figure
3
.
The x-axis in all figures is
|\log_{10}\alpha|
. On the other hand, in each figure, the y-axis is the expected sample size of the tests in part (a), the ratio of the expected sample size of
the versions without prior information with respect to that of the version with bigger subsystem as well as prior information in part (b), and the ratio of expected sample size of all tests over the first-order approximation of the corresponding optimal performance in part (c).

From parts (a) and (b) we see, not surprisingly, that the expected sample size is smaller when using bigger subsystems and utilize prior information, and that this effect is more substantial in the second study and when
\alpha
is small. Moreover, we see that parts (b) and (c)
are consistent with the asymptotic optimality results mentioned above, according to which all versions of
\chi^{*}
are asymptotic optimal in the first study, and only those with the subsystems of size 3 in the second study.
The Monte Carlo standard error in the estimation of every expectation did not exceed
2\%
of the corresponding estimate.

(b)

(c)

Fig 2:
Performance of the tests under the first study, where
K=7
.

(a)

(b)

(c)

Fig 3:

Performance of the tests under the second study, where
K=3
.

8
Conclusion

In the present paper we consider a setup where multiple data streams are generated by distinct data sources and we propose a novel multiple testing formulation that unifies the problems of detection and isolation. Specifically, we introduce a family of testing procedures, of various computational complexities, that control 4 distinct error metrics, two of which are related to the pure detection problem and two to the pure isolation problem. We characterize the optimal expected sample size in the class of all sequential tests that control these error probabilities to a first-order asymptotic approximation as the latter go to 0. Moreover,
we evaluate the asymptotic relative efficiency of low-complexity rules in the proposed family, and establish sufficient conditions for their asymptotic optimality.

Unlike most previous works in the literature, the results in the present paper do not rely on the assumption of independence among the data sources. Thus, we apply them to the problem of detecting and isolating (i) anomalous, possibly dependent, data streams, (ii) an unknown dependence structure. For the first of these problems, in the case of independence we obtain asymptotically optimal rules that are easily implementable in practice, under general conditions that allow for temporal dependence. Thus, we generalize previous results in the literature, which focus either on the pure detection or the pure isolation problem.

There are various natural generalizations of the present work. For example, we can obtain a similar asymptotic theory using alternative metrics for the false positive and false negative rates, such as FDR, pFDR, etc., working similarly to
[
19
]
. Moreover, we can extend the results of the current work to the case that the distribution of each unit under each hypothesis belongs to a parametric family, working similarly to
[
35
, Section 6]
.

Finally, there are many open questions and directions for further research, such as a more precise description of the optimal performance, the proof of stronger optimality properties,
asymptotic regimes where the number of sources also goes to infinity as the error probabilities vanish, modeling the data streams using spatial models, e.g., a Markov random field, where the special underlying dependence can lead to further insights,
closed-form expressions for the thresholds that are less conservative than the ones we obtain in this work, more efficient design of subsystems, etc. Finally, another direction of interest is to allow for the sampling to be terminated at a different time in each data stream, as in
[
4
,
7
,
29
]
.

†
†
funding:
This work was supported in part by NSF Grant DMS-1737962.

A
Proofs regarding Error Control

A.1
Some important lemmas

In this subsection we establish some lemmas which are critical in establishing the error control in Theorems
3.1
and
3.2
.

Lemma A.1
.

Suppose
\emptyset\in\Psi
. Fix any
e\in\mathcal{K}
and
D_{e}>1
. If
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
then

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm det}\left(n\right)\geq D_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)\leq\frac{|\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s_{e}}|}{D_{e}}.

We have,

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm det}\left(n\right)\geq D_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle={\sf P}^{s_{e}}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm det}\left(n\right)\geq D_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\sf P}^{s_{e}}\left(\max_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s_{e}}}\frac{\Lambda_{n}({\sf Q},{\sf P}_{0})}{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P}^{s_{e}},{\sf P}_{0})}\geq D_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right),\ \text{since}\ \;\;{\sf P}^{s_{e}}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}^{s_{e}}

\displaystyle\leq{\sf P}^{s_{e}}\left(\bigcup_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s_{e}}}\left\{\frac{\Lambda_{n}({\sf Q},{\sf P}_{0})}{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P}^{s_{e}},{\sf P}_{0})}\geq D_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s_{e}}}{\sf P}^{s_{e}}\left(\Lambda_{n}({\sf Q},{\sf P}^{s_{e}})\geq D_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s_{e}}}\frac{1}{D_{e}}=\frac{|\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s_{e}}|}{D_{e}},

where the third inequality follows from Boole’s inequality and the last one follows from Ville’s inequality.
∎

Lemma A.2
.

Suppose
\emptyset\in\Psi
. Fix any
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}
,
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
and
C_{e}>1
. Then

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm det}\left(n\right)\leq\frac{1}{C_{e}}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)\leq\frac{|\mathcal{H}_{0}^{s_{e}}|}{C_{e}}.

Since
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
and
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}
, we have
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}
. Now,

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm det}\left(n\right)\leq\frac{1}{C_{e}}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle={\sf P}^{s_{e}}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm det}\left(n\right)\leq\frac{1}{C_{e}}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\sf P}^{s_{e}}\left(\max_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}^{s_{e}}}\frac{\Lambda_{n}({\sf Q},{\sf P}_{0})}{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P}^{s_{e}},{\sf P}_{0})}\geq C_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right),\ \text{since}\ \;\;{\sf P}^{s_{e}}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s_{e}}

\displaystyle\leq{\sf P}^{s_{e}}\left(\bigcup_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}^{s_{e}}}\left\{\frac{\Lambda_{n}({\sf Q},{\sf P}_{0})}{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P}^{s_{e}},{\sf P}_{0})}\geq C_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}^{s_{e}}}{\sf P}^{s_{e}}\left(\Lambda_{n}({\sf Q},{\sf P}^{s_{e}})\geq C_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}^{s_{e}}}\frac{1}{C_{e}}=\frac{|\mathcal{H}_{0}^{s_{e}}|}{C_{e}},

where the third inequality follows from Boole’s inequality and the last one follows from Ville’s inequality.
∎

Lemma A.3
.

Suppose
\emptyset\in\Psi
.
Fix any
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}
,
e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
and
B_{e}>1
. Then

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}\left(n\right)\geq B_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)\leq\frac{|\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}|}{B_{e}}.

Since
e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
and
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}
, we have
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}
. Now,

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}\left(n\right)\geq B_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle={\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}\left(n\right)\geq B_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\left(\max_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}}\frac{\Lambda_{n}({\sf Q},{\sf P}_{0})}{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}},{\sf P}_{0})}\geq B_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right),\ \text{since}\ \;\;{\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}

\displaystyle\leq{\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\left(\bigcup_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}}\left\{\frac{\Lambda_{n}({\sf Q},{\sf P}_{0})}{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}},{\sf P}_{0})}\geq B_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}}{\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\left(\Lambda_{n}({\sf Q},{\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}})\geq B_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}}\frac{1}{B_{e}}=\frac{|\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}|}{B_{e}},

where the third inequality follows from Boole’s inequality and the last one follows from Ville’s inequality.
∎

Lemma A.4
.

Suppose
\emptyset\in\Psi
.
Fix any
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}
,
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
and
A_{e}>1
. Then

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}\left(n\right)\leq\frac{1}{A_{e}}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)\leq\frac{|\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}|}{A_{e}}.

Since
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
and
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}
, we have
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}
. Now,

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}\left(n\right)\leq\frac{1}{A_{e}}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle={\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}\left(n\right)\leq\frac{1}{A_{e}}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\left(\max_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}}\frac{\Lambda_{n}({\sf Q},{\sf P}_{0})}{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}},{\sf P}_{0})}\geq A_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right),\ \text{since}\ \;\;{\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}

\displaystyle\leq{\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\left(\bigcup_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}}\left\{\frac{\Lambda_{n}({\sf Q},{\sf P}_{0})}{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}},{\sf P}_{0})}\geq A_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}}{\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\left(\Lambda_{n}({\sf Q},{\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}})\geq A_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}}\frac{1}{A_{e}}=\frac{|\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}|}{A_{e}},

where the third inequality follows from Boole’s inequality and the last one follows from Ville’s inequality.
∎

A.2
Proof of Theorem
3.1

The first part of the proof is similar to the proof of Theorem
3.2
(iii), which is done later.

For the second part, note that when
\Psi=\Psi_{m,m}
for some
0<m<|\mathcal{K}|
, then for any
(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)
, we have

{\sf P}\left(D\setminus\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\neq\emptyset\right)={\sf P}\left(\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\setminus D\neq\emptyset\right)={\sf P}\left(D\neq\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\right)\leq\gamma\wedge\delta
.
Therefore, replacing both
\gamma
and
\delta
by
\gamma\wedge\delta
in the lower bounds of
A_{e}
and
B_{e}
gives us the result.
∎

A.3
Proof of Theorem
3.2

First, we prove (iii) since the proof of (i) follows similarly. Fix the thresholds
\{A_{e},B_{e},C_{e},D_{e}\}_{e\in\mathcal{K}}>1
. We detect that there is at least one unit in which alternative is correct only when
T_{joint}<T_{0}
which means
\left\{\Lambda_{e,\rm det}\left(T^{*}\right)\geq D_{e}\right\}
for some
e\in\mathcal{K}
. Formally,

\left\{D^{*}\neq\emptyset\right\}\subseteq\bigcup_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\left\{\Lambda_{e,\rm det}\left(T^{*}\right)\geq D_{e}\right\}.

Now, consider any arbitrary
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(D^{*}\neq\emptyset\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{K}}{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm det}\left(T^{*}\right)\geq D_{e}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{K}}{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm det}\left(n\right)\geq D_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\frac{|\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s_{e}}|}{D_{e}}

where the first inequality follows from Boole’s inequality and the third one follows from Lemma
A.1
.
Thus, if we select
D_{e}
according to (i), the probability of false alarm is controlled.

For the rest of the proof of (iii), consider any arbitrary
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}
.
We detect that null hypothesis is true in all units only if
T_{0}<T_{joint}
, which means
\left\{\Lambda_{e,\rm det}\left(T^{*}\right)\leq\frac{1}{C_{e}}\right\}
for all
e\in\mathcal{K}
, and therefore, for
any arbitrary
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
. Formally,

\left\{D^{*}=\emptyset\right\}\subseteq\left\{\Lambda_{e,\rm det}\left(T^{*}\right)\leq\frac{1}{C_{e}}\right\},

which implies

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(D^{*}=\emptyset\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm det}\left(T^{*}\right)\leq\frac{1}{C_{e}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm det}\left(n\right)\leq\frac{1}{C_{e}}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|\mathcal{H}_{0}^{s_{e}}|}{C_{e}},

where the third inequality follows from Lemma
A.2
.
Thus, if we select
C_{e}
according to (i), the probability of missed detection is controlled.

Note that, at least one false positive is made only if
T_{joint}<T_{0}
and we mistakenly isolate a unit in which null is true. Formally, the event
\left\{\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}(T^{*})\geq B_{e}\right\}
happens for some
e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
, i.e.,

\left\{D^{*}\setminus\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\neq\emptyset\right\}\subseteq\bigcup_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}(T^{*})\geq B_{e}\right\},

which implies

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(D^{*}\setminus\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\neq\emptyset\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}\left(T^{*}\right)\geq B_{e}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}\left(n\right)\geq B_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\frac{|\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}|}{B_{e}}\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\frac{|\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}|}{B_{e}},

where the first inequality follows from Boole’s inequality and the third one follows from Lemma
A.3
.
Thus, if we select
B_{e}
as in Theorem
3.1
we obtain the desired error control.

Next, observe that at least one false negative is made
and

D^{*}\neq\emptyset
only if
T_{joint}<T_{0}
and we fail to isolate a unit in which alternative is true. Formally, the event
\left\{\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}(T^{*})\leq\frac{1}{A_{e}}\right\}
happens for some
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
, i.e.,

\left\{\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\setminus D^{*}\neq\emptyset,D^{*}\neq\emptyset\right\}\subseteq\bigcup_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}(T^{*})\leq\frac{1}{A_{e}}\right\},

which implies,

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\setminus D^{*}\neq\emptyset,D^{*}\neq\emptyset\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}\left(T^{*}\right)\leq\frac{1}{A_{e}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}\left(n\right)\leq\frac{1}{A_{e}}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\frac{|\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}|}{A_{e}}\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\frac{|\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}|}{A_{e}}

where the first inequality follows from Boole’s inequality and the third one follows from Lemma
A.4
.
Thus, if we select
A_{e}
as in Theorem
3.1
we obtain the desired error control.

Next, we prove (ii) regarding
\chi^{*}_{fwer}
, where the thresholds in
\chi^{*}
are selected such that
A_{e}=C_{e}
and
B_{e}=D_{e}
for every
e\in\mathcal{K}
. Fix any arbitrary
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}
.
Note that, at least one false positive is made if there exists some
e\in D^{*}_{fwer}\setminus\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
. Now,
e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
means either
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
or
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}
. Suppose the first case holds, that is,
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
. Then,

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(D^{*}_{fwer}\setminus\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\neq\emptyset\right)

\displaystyle={\sf P}\left(D^{*}_{fwer}\neq\emptyset\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{K}}{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm det}\left(T^{*}_{fwer}\right)\geq B_{e}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{K}}{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm det}\left(n\right)\geq B_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\frac{|\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s_{e}}|}{B_{e}}

(25)

where the first inequality follows from Boole’s inequality and the third one follows from Lemma
A.1
.
If the second case holds, that is,
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}
, then

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(D^{*}_{fwer}\setminus\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\neq\emptyset\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}(T^{*}_{fwer})\geq B_{e}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}\left(n\right)\geq B_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\frac{|\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}|}{B_{e}}\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\frac{|\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}|}{B_{e}},

(26)

where the first inequality follows from Boole’s inequality and the third one follows from Lemma
A.3
. Then, from (
25
) and (
26
), we have

{\sf P}\left(D^{*}_{fwer}\setminus\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\neq\emptyset\right)\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\frac{|\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s_{e}}|\vee|\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}|}{B_{e}}.

Thus, if we select
B_{e}
according to (ii), we obtain the desired error control.

Next, observe that at least one false negative is made if either
T_{0}<T_{joint}
and we simply declare
D^{*}_{fwer}=\emptyset
, or
T_{joint}<T_{0}
and we incur the event
\{\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\setminus D^{*}_{fwer}\neq\emptyset,D^{*}_{fwer}\neq\emptyset\}
. Formally,

\{\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\setminus D^{*}_{fwer}\neq\emptyset\}=\{D^{*}_{fwer}=\emptyset\}\cup\{\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\setminus D^{*}_{fwer}\neq\emptyset,D^{*}_{fwer}\neq\emptyset\}.

For any
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
, the probability of the first event can be bounded as follows:

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(D^{*}_{fwer}=\emptyset\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm det}\left(T^{*}_{fwer}\right)\leq\frac{1}{A_{e}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm det}\left(n\right)\leq\frac{1}{A_{e}}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|\mathcal{H}_{0}^{s_{e}}|}{A_{e}},

(27)

where the third inequality follows from Lemma
A.2
. Furthermore, for the second event, we have

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\setminus D^{*}_{fwer}\neq\emptyset,D^{*}_{fwer}\neq\emptyset\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}\left(T^{*}_{fwer}\right)\leq\frac{1}{A_{e}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}\left(n\right)\leq\frac{1}{A_{e}}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\frac{|\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}|}{A_{e}}\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\frac{|\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}|}{A_{e}},

(28)

where the third inequality follows from Lemma
A.4
.
Then from (
27
) and (
28
), we have

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\setminus D^{*}_{fwer}\neq\emptyset\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|\mathcal{H}_{0}^{s_{e}}|}{A_{e}}+\sum_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\frac{|\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}|}{A_{e}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{|\mathcal{H}_{0}^{s_{e}}|\vee|\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}|}{A_{e}}+\sum_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\frac{|\mathcal{H}_{0}^{s_{e}}|\vee|\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}|}{A_{e}}.

Thus, if we select
A_{e}
according to (ii), we obtain the desired error control.

Further, note that, for any
e\in\mathcal{K}
if
s_{e}=s^{\prime}_{e}=e
then
b_{e}=d_{e}=|\mathcal{G}^{e}|
, and
a_{e}=c_{e}=|\mathcal{H}^{e}|
. Thus,
B_{e}\geq(b_{e}\vee d_{e})|\mathcal{K}|/\gamma=b_{e}|\mathcal{K}|/\gamma
.
On the other hand, one can bound the probability of at least one false negative as follows.

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\setminus D^{*}_{fwer}\neq\emptyset\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e}(T^{*}_{fwer})\leq\frac{1}{A_{e}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}{\sf P}\left(\Lambda_{e}(n)\leq\frac{1}{A_{e}}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;n\in\mathbb{N}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\frac{|\mathcal{H}^{e}|}{A_{e}}\leq\sum_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\frac{a_{e}}{A_{e}}

where the first inequality follows from Boole’s inequality and the last one follows form Lemma
A.4
. Thus, if we select
A_{e}
according to (ii), we obtain the desired error control. The proof is complete.
∎

B
Some properties regarding the Information Numbers

In this section, we present some important lemmas about the information numbers introduced in Section
4
, which are critical to establishing Proposition
D.1
in Appendix
D
as well as may be of independent interest to the reader.

Lemma B.1
.

Suppose that (
11
) holds for two distinct probability distributions
{\sf P},{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{P}
, when
s=s_{1},s_{2}\subseteq[K]
.

\text{If}\quad s_{1}\subseteq s_{2},\quad\text{then}\quad\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{1})\leq\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{2}\right).

Consider the hypotheses testing problem

H_{0}:X\sim{\sf P}\quad\text{versus}\quad H_{1}:X\sim{\sf Q},

where we control both type 1 error and type 2 error below
\alpha
, for some
\alpha\in(0,1)
.
A test, in this context, is a pair
(T,d)
that consists of an
\{\mathcal{F}_{n}\}
-stopping time
T
that represents the time at which the decision is taken and an
\mathcal{F}_{T}
-measurable bernoulli random variable
d
, such that
\{d=i\}
represents hypothesis
H_{i}
is selected upon stopping,
i=0,1
.
Let
\mathcal{C}^{s}
be the class of sequential procedures
(T,d)
which satisfy the desired error control based on
\mathcal{F}^{s}
, that is, observations only from
s
. Since
s_{1}\subseteq s_{2}
, we have
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{s_{1}}\subseteq\mathcal{F}_{n}^{s_{2}}
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
, which implies
\mathcal{F}^{s_{1}}\subseteq\mathcal{F}^{s_{2}}
and therefore,
\mathcal{C}^{s_{1}}\subseteq\mathcal{C}^{s_{2}}
.
This leads to

\inf_{(T,d)\in\mathcal{C}^{s_{1}}}{\sf E}_{\sf P}\left[T\right]\geq\inf_{(T,d)\in\mathcal{C}^{s_{2}}}{\sf E}_{\sf P}\left[T\right].

(29)

Now, from
[
37
, Lemma 3.4.1]
, as
\alpha\to 0
, we have

\inf_{(T,d)\in\mathcal{C}^{s}}{\sf E}_{\sf P}\left[T\right]\sim\frac{|\log\alpha|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s\right)},\quad s=s_{1},s_{2},

which combining with (
29
) completes the proof.
∎

Lemma B.2
.

Consider
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}
and class of probability distributions
\mathcal{Q}\subset\mathcal{P}
, where
{\sf P}\notin\mathcal{Q}
.
Suppose that (
11
) holds for
{\sf P}
and every
{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{Q}
, when
s=s_{1},s_{2}\subseteq[K]
.

\text{If}\quad s_{1}\subseteq s_{2},\quad\text{then}\quad\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{Q};s_{1})\leq\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{Q};s_{2}\right).

From Lemma
B.1
, for every
{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{Q}
, we have

\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{1})\leq\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{2}).

Therefore, taking minimum over the class
\mathcal{Q}
on both sides, we obtain

\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{Q};s_{1}\right)=\min_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{Q}}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{1}\right)\leq\min_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{Q}}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{2}\right)=\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{Q};s_{2}\right),

and the proof is complete.
∎

Lemma B.3
.

Consider two disjoint subsets
s_{1},s_{2}\subset[K]
.
Suppose that (
11
) and (
12
) hold for two distinct probability distribution
{\sf P},{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{P}
when
s=s_{1},s_{2}
. If
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
are independent both under
{\sf P}
and
{\sf Q}
, then (
11
) and (
12
) hold for
{\sf P}
,
{\sf Q}
and
s=s_{1}\cup s_{2}
with

\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q};s)=\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{1}\right)+\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{2}\right).

(30)

Let
s=s_{1}\cup s_{2}
.
Since
s_{1}
and
s_{2}
are independent both under
{\sf P}
and
{\sf Q}
, we have

{\sf P}^{s}={\sf P}^{s_{1}}\otimes{\sf P}^{s_{2}},\quad\text{and}\quad{\sf Q}^{s}={\sf Q}^{s_{1}}\otimes{\sf Q}^{s_{2}}.

Now, suppose for a measurable set
A
,
{\sf Q}^{s}(A)=0
, which means

\displaystyle{\sf Q}^{s_{1}}(A)\times{\sf Q}^{s_{2}}(A)=0

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\;\;

\displaystyle{\sf Q}^{s_{1}}(A)=0\;\;\text{or},\;\;{\sf Q}^{s_{2}}(A)=0.

By the definition of absolute continuity, if the first case happens, then
{\sf P}^{s_{1}}(A)=0
, otherwise,
{\sf P}^{s_{2}}(A)=0
. Anyway, both cases lead to
{\sf P}^{s}(A)={\sf P}^{s_{1}}(A)\times{\sf P}^{s_{2}}(A)=0
. An exactly similar argument also shows that if
{\sf P}(A)=0
, then
{\sf Q}(A)=0
.
Therefore,
{\sf P}
and
{\sf Q}
are mutually absolutely continuous when restricted to
\mathcal{F}_{n}^{s}
.
Furthermore, we have the following decomposition of the likelihood process
\Lambda_{n}\left({\sf P}^{s},{\sf Q}^{s}\right)
:

\displaystyle\Lambda_{n}\left({\sf P}^{s},{\sf Q}^{s}\right)=\frac{d{\sf P}^{s_{1}}\otimes d{\sf P}^{s_{2}}}{d{\sf Q}^{s_{1}}\otimes d{\sf Q}^{s_{2}}}\left(\mathcal{F}_{n}^{s}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{d{\sf P}^{s_{1}}}{d{\sf Q}^{s_{1}}}\left(\mathcal{F}_{n}^{s_{1}}\right)\times\frac{d{\sf P}^{s_{2}}}{d{\sf Q}^{s_{2}}}\left(\mathcal{F}_{n}^{s_{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{n}\left({\sf P}^{s_{1}},{\sf Q}^{s_{1}}\right)\times\Lambda_{n}\left({\sf P}^{s_{2}},{\sf Q}^{s_{2}}\right),

which subsequently leads us to

Z_{n}\left({\sf P}^{s},{\sf Q}^{s}\right)=Z_{n}\left({\sf P}^{s_{1}},{\sf Q}^{s_{1}}\right)+Z_{n}\left({\sf P}^{s_{2}},{\sf Q}^{s_{2}}\right).

Now, in order to prove (
30
), it suffices to show that

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq n}Z_{k}\left({\sf P}^{s},{\sf Q}^{s}\right)\geq n\rho\right)\to 0\quad\forall\,\rho>\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{1}\right)+\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{2}\right),

(31)

and at the same time

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}{\sf P}\left(Z_{n}\left({\sf P}^{s},{\sf Q}^{s}\right)<n\rho\right)<\infty\quad\forall\,\rho<\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{1}\right)+\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{2}\right),

(32)

since these two conditions uniquely characterize the quantity
\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q};s)
.

To start with, we fix an arbitrary
\rho>\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{1}\right)+\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{2}\right)
. Note that, there exist
\rho_{1}>\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{1}\right)
and
\rho_{2}>\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{2}\right)
such that
\rho=\rho_{1}+\rho_{2}
. Indeed, by letting
\delta=\rho-(\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{1}\right)+\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{2}\right))>0
, one can consider
\rho_{1}=\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{1}\right)+\delta/2
and
\rho_{2}=\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{2}\right)+\delta/2
. Now,

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq n}Z_{k}\left({\sf P}^{s},{\sf Q}^{s}\right)\geq n\rho\right)

\displaystyle={\sf P}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq n}\left\{Z_{k}\left({\sf P}^{s_{1}},{\sf Q}^{s_{1}}\right)+Z_{k}\left({\sf P}^{s_{2}},{\sf Q}^{s_{2}}\right)\right\}\geq n\rho\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\sf P}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq n}Z_{k}\left({\sf P}^{s_{1}},{\sf Q}^{s_{1}}\right)+\max_{1\leq k\leq n}Z_{k}\left({\sf P}^{s_{2}},{\sf Q}^{s_{2}}\right)\geq n\rho_{1}+n\rho_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\sf P}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq n}Z_{k}\left({\sf P}^{s_{1}},{\sf Q}^{s_{1}}\right)\geq n\rho_{1}\right)+{\sf P}\left(\max_{1\leq k\leq n}Z_{k}\left({\sf P}^{s_{2}},{\sf Q}^{s_{2}}\right)\geq n\rho_{2}\right),

(33)

where the first inequality follows from the fact that, for any two sequences
\{a_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{b_{n}\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
,

\max_{1\leq k\leq n}\left\{a_{k}+b_{k}\right\}\leq\max_{1\leq k\leq n}a_{k}+\max_{1\leq k\leq n}b_{k},\quad\text{for every}\;\;n\in\mathbb{N},

and the last inequality is an application of Boole’s inequality. Since
\rho_{1}>\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{1}\right)
and
\rho_{2}>\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{2}\right)
, due to condition (
11
), both the quantities on the right hand side of (
33
) converge to
0
as
n\to\infty
. Hence, (
31
) follows.

In order to prove (
32
), we fix an arbitrary
\rho<\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{1}\right)+\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{2}\right)
. Note that, in a similar way like the previous, there exist
\rho_{1}<\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{1}\right)
and
\rho_{2}<\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{2}\right)
such that
\rho=\rho_{1}+\rho_{2}
. Now,

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}{\sf P}\left(Z_{n}\left({\sf P}^{s},{\sf Q}^{s}\right)<n\rho\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}{\sf P}\left(\left\{Z_{n}\left({\sf P}^{s_{1}},{\sf Q}^{s_{1}}\right)+Z_{n}\left({\sf P}^{s_{2}},{\sf Q}^{s_{2}}\right)\right\}<n\rho_{1}+n\rho_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}{\sf P}\left(Z_{n}\left({\sf P}^{s_{1}},{\sf Q}^{s_{1}}\right)<n\rho_{1}\right)+{\sf P}\left(Z_{n}\left({\sf P}^{s_{2}},{\sf Q}^{s_{2}}\right)<n\rho_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}{\sf P}\left(Z_{n}\left({\sf P}^{s_{1}},{\sf Q}^{s_{1}}\right)<n\rho_{1}\right)+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}{\sf P}\left(Z_{n}\left({\sf P}^{s_{2}},{\sf Q}^{s_{2}}\right)<n\rho_{2}\right),

(34)

where the inequality follows from Boole’s inequality. Since
\rho_{1}<\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{1}\right)
and
\rho_{2}<\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{2}\right)
, due to condition (
12
), both the quantities on the right hand side of (
34
) are finite. Hence, (
32
) follows and the proof is complete.

∎

C
Proofs regarding Upper Bound on the expected sample size

In this section we only prove Theorem
4.2
since Theorem
4.1
can be established in a similar fashion.

C.1
Proof of Theorem
4.2

It is sufficient to prove the results only for
\chi^{*}
since it can be proved for the other tests in a similar way.
Fix the thresholds
A_{e},B_{e},C_{e},D_{e}
for all
e\in\mathcal{K}
according to (
8
) and (
9
). Now for any
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
, consider the stopping time

\displaystyle T_{\sf P}:=\inf\left\{n\geq 1:\min\left\{\Lambda_{n}\left({\sf P}^{s_{e}},{\sf Q}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s_{e}}\right\}\geq C_{e}\ \;\;\text{for every}\ \;\;e\in\mathcal{K}\right\}.

Under the assumption (
12
), from
[
36
, Lemma F.2]
, it follows that as
\beta\to 0
,

{\sf E}_{\sf P}\left[T_{\sf P}\right]\;\lesssim\;\max_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\left\{\frac{|\log\beta|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e};s_{e}\right)}\right\}.

Since
T^{*}\leq T_{0}
, it suffices to show that
T_{0}\leq T_{\sf P}
for any given set of thresholds
\{C_{e}:e\in\mathcal{K}\}
.
By the definition of
T_{0}
, it suffices to show that

\Lambda_{e,\rm det}(T_{\sf P})\leq\frac{1}{C_{e}}\ \;\;\text{for every}\ \;\;e\in\mathcal{K}.

(35)

To this end, fix any arbitrary
e\in\mathcal{K}
and
by the definition of
T_{\sf P}
, we have

\min\left\{\Lambda_{T_{\sf P}}\left({\sf P}^{s_{e}},{\sf Q}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s_{e}}\right\}\geq C_{e}.

As a result,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{C_{e}}\geq\max\left\{\Lambda_{T_{\sf P}}\left({\sf Q},{\sf P}^{s_{e}}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s_{e}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{\max\left\{\Lambda_{T_{\sf P}}\left({\sf Q},{\sf P}_{0}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s_{e}}\right\}}{\Lambda_{T_{\sf P}}\left({\sf P}^{s_{e}},{\sf P}_{0}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\max\left\{\Lambda_{T_{\sf P}}\left({\sf Q},{\sf P}_{0}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s_{e}}\right\}}{\max\left\{\Lambda_{T_{\sf P}}\left({\sf Q},{\sf P}_{0}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}^{s_{e}}\right\}}

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{e,\rm det}(T_{\sf P}),

and the result follows in view of (
35
).

Now for any
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}
, consider the stopping time

\displaystyle T^{\prime}_{\sf P}:=\inf\bigg\{n\geq 1:

\displaystyle\min\left\{\Lambda_{n}\left({\sf P}^{s_{e}},{\sf Q}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}^{s_{e}}\right\}\geq D_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P}),

\displaystyle\min\left\{\Lambda_{n}\left({\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}},{\sf Q}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\right\}\geq B_{e}\ \;\;\text{for every}\ \;\;e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P}),\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\min\left\{\Lambda_{n}\left({\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}},{\sf Q}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\right\}\geq A_{e}\ \;\;\text{for every}\ \;\;e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\bigg\}.

Under the assumption (
12
), from
[
36
, Lemma F.2]
, it follows that as
\alpha,\gamma,\delta\to 0
,

{\sf E}_{\sf P}\left[T^{\prime}_{{\sf P}}\right]\lesssim\min_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\alpha|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0};s_{e}\right)}\right\}\bigvee\max_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\gamma|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e};s^{\prime}_{e}\right)}\right\}\bigvee\max_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\delta|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e};s^{\prime}_{e}\right)}\right\}.

Since
T^{*}\leq T_{joint}
, it suffices to show that
T_{joint}\leq T^{\prime}_{{\sf P}}
for any given set of thresholds
\{A_{e},B_{e},D_{e}:e\in\mathcal{K}\}
. By the definition of
T_{joint}
it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Lambda_{e,\rm det}\left(T^{\prime}_{\sf P}\right)\geq D_{e}\ \;\;\text{for some}\ \;\;e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P}),\\
&\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}\left(T^{\prime}_{\sf P}\right)\geq B_{e}\ \;\;\text{for every}\ \;\;e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P}),\\
&\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}\left(T^{\prime}_{\sf P}\right)\leq 1/A_{e}\ \;\;\text{for every}\ e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P}),\\
\text{and}\ \;\;&D_{\rm iso}\left(T^{\prime}_{\sf P}\right)\in\Psi.\end{split}

(36)

Now, note that if
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
, which means under
{\sf P}
alternative is true in unit
e
, then
{\sf P}^{s}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s}
for
s\in\{s_{e},s^{\prime}_{e}\}
.

To this end, consider that particular
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
for which by definition of
T^{\prime}_{\sf P}
,

\min\left\{\Lambda_{T^{\prime}_{\sf P}}\left({\sf P}^{s_{e}},{\sf Q}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}^{s_{e}}\right\}\geq D_{e}.

As a result,

\displaystyle D_{e}\leq\min\left\{\Lambda_{T^{\prime}_{\sf P}}\left({\sf P}^{s_{e}},{\sf Q}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}^{s_{e}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda_{T^{\prime}_{\sf P}}\left({\sf P}^{s_{e}},{\sf P}_{0}\right)}{\max\left\{\Lambda_{T^{\prime}_{\sf P}}\left({\sf Q},{\sf P}_{0}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}^{s_{e}}\right\}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\max\left\{\Lambda_{T^{\prime}_{\sf P}}\left({\sf Q},{\sf P}_{0}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s_{e}}\right\}}{\max\left\{\Lambda_{T^{\prime}_{\sf P}}\left({\sf Q},{\sf P}_{0}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}^{s_{e}}\right\}}=\Lambda_{e,\rm det}(T^{\prime}_{\sf P}),

where the last inequality follows because
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}
.

Now fix any arbitrary
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
. Then
by definition of
T^{\prime}_{\sf P}
, we have

\min\left\{\Lambda_{T^{\prime}_{\sf P}}\left({\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}},{\sf Q}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\right\}\geq B_{e}.

As a result,

\displaystyle B_{e}\leq\min\left\{\Lambda_{T^{\prime}_{\sf P}}\left({\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}},{\sf Q}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Lambda_{T^{\prime}_{\sf P}}\left({\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}},{\sf P}_{0}\right)}{\max\left\{\Lambda_{T^{\prime}_{\sf P}}\left({\sf Q},{\sf P}_{0}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\right\}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\max\left\{\Lambda_{T^{\prime}_{\sf P}}\left({\sf Q},{\sf P}_{0}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\right\}}{\max\left\{\Lambda_{T^{\prime}_{\sf P}}\left({\sf Q},{\sf P}_{0}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\right\}}=\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}(T^{\prime}_{\sf P}),

where the last inequality follows because
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}
.

Next, fix any arbitrary
e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
. Then by definition of
T^{\prime}_{\sf P}
, we have

\min\left\{\Lambda_{T^{\prime}_{\sf P}}\left({\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}},{\sf Q}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\right\}\geq A_{e}.

As a result,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{A_{e}}\geq\max\left\{\Lambda_{T^{\prime}_{\sf P}}\left({\sf Q},{\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{\max\left\{\Lambda_{T^{\prime}_{\sf P}}\left({\sf Q},{\sf P}_{0}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\right\}}{\Lambda_{T^{\prime}_{\sf P}}\left({\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}},{\sf P}_{0}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\max\left\{\Lambda_{T^{\prime}_{\sf P}}\left({\sf Q},{\sf P}_{0}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\right\}}{\max\left\{\Lambda_{T^{\prime}_{\sf P}}\left({\sf Q},{\sf P}_{0}\right):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}\right\}}=\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}(T^{\prime}_{\sf P}),

where the last inequality follows because
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}
. Therefore,

\Lambda_{e,\rm iso}(T^{\prime}_{\sf P})>1\quad\text{if and only if}\quad e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P}),

which implies that
D_{\rm iso}(T^{\prime}_{{\sf P}})=\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\in\Psi
.
Thus, the proof is complete in view of (
36
).
∎

D
Proofs regarding Asymptotic Optimality

For any random variable
Y
with underlying probability distribution
{\sf P}
and any event
\Gamma
, we introduce the following notation:

{\sf E}_{\sf P}\left[Y;\Gamma\right]:=\int_{\Gamma}Yd{\sf P},

which will be extensively used in this section.

D.1
Proof of Theorem
4.3

First, we establish a lemma which is critical in establishing an asymptotic lower bound on the optimal performance.

Lemma D.1
.

Suppose that (
11
) holds,
\emptyset\notin\Psi
and
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}
.
Then as
\gamma,\delta\to 0
we have

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)\right\}\;\gtrsim\;\max_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\gamma|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e})}\right\}\bigvee\max_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\delta|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e})}\right\}.

When in particular
\Psi=\Psi_{m,m}
for some
0<m<|\mathcal{K}|
,
then as
\gamma,\delta\to 0
we have

\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)\right\}\;\gtrsim\;\frac{|\log(\gamma\wedge\delta)|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\{{\sf P}\})}.

Fix
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}
and denote the quantity
\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)\right\}
by
N(\gamma,\delta)
. Then we need to show that as
\gamma,\delta\to 0
,

N(\gamma,\delta)\gtrsim\max_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\gamma|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e})}\right\}\bigvee\max_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\delta|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e})}\right\}.

We set

L_{\gamma}:=\max_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\gamma|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e})}\right\},\quad\text{and}\quad L_{\delta}:=\max_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\delta|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e})}\right\}.

By Markov’s inequality, for any stopping time
T
and
\gamma,\delta,\epsilon\in(0,1)
,

{\sf E}_{\sf P}[T]\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}{\sf P}\left(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad{\sf E}_{\sf P}[T]\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}{\sf P}\left(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}\right).

Thus, it suffices to show for every
\epsilon\in(0,1)
we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{\gamma,\delta\to 0}\inf_{(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)}{\sf P}\left(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}\right)\geq 1,\quad\text{and}

(37a)

\displaystyle\liminf_{\gamma,\delta\to 0}\inf_{(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)}{\sf P}\left(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}\right)\geq 1,

(37b)

since this will further imply that

\liminf_{\gamma,\delta\to 0}\;N(\gamma,\delta)/(L_{\gamma}\vee L_{\delta})\geq(1-\epsilon),

and the desired result will follow by letting
\epsilon\to 0
.
In order to prove (
37
), we start by fixing arbitrary
(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)
and
\gamma,\delta,\epsilon\in(0,1)
.
To show (
37a
), consider the probability distribution
{\sf Q}_{1}^{*}
which attains

\min\left\{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q}):{\sf Q}\in\cup_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}\right\}.

Thus, there exists
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
such that
{\sf Q}_{1}^{*}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}
, i.e.,
e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf Q}_{1}^{*})
. Then,

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle\geq{\sf P}\left(e\in D,T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle={\sf P}\left(e\in D\right)-{\sf P}\left(e\in D,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle=1-{\sf P}\left(e\notin D\right)-{\sf P}\left(e\in D,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\delta-{\sf P}\left(e\in D,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}\right)

(38)

where the last inequality follows from the fact that

\{e\notin D\}\subseteq\{\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\setminus D\neq\emptyset,D\neq\emptyset\}

and the error control that
{\sf P}\left(\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\setminus D\neq\emptyset,D\neq\emptyset\right)\leq\delta
. We can now decompose the third term as

{\sf P}\left(e\in D,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}\right)\leq p_{1}+p_{2},

such that

\displaystyle p_{1}

\displaystyle:={\sf P}\left(e\in D,\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{1}^{*})<\frac{\eta}{\gamma}\right)\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle p_{2}

\displaystyle:={\sf P}\left(T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma},\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{1}^{*})\geq\frac{\eta}{\gamma}\right),

where
\eta
is an arbitrary constant in
(0,1)
. For the first term, by a change of measure from
{\sf P}
to
{\sf Q}_{1}^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle p_{1}

\displaystyle={\sf E}_{{\sf Q}_{1}^{*}}\left[\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{1}^{*});e\in D,\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{1}^{*})<\frac{\eta}{\gamma}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta}{\gamma}{\sf Q}_{1}^{*}\left(e\in D\right)\leq\eta,

where the last inequality follows from the fact that

\{e\in D\}\subseteq\{D\setminus\mathcal{A}({\sf Q}_{1}^{*})\neq\emptyset\}

and the error control that
{\sf Q}_{1}^{*}\left(D\setminus\mathcal{A}({\sf Q}_{1}^{*})\neq\emptyset\right)\leq\gamma
. For the second term,

\displaystyle p_{2}\leq{\sf P}\left(\frac{1}{(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}}\max_{1\leq n\leq(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}}\log\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{1}^{*})\geq\frac{\log\eta}{(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}}+\frac{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{1}^{*})}{(1-\epsilon)}\right)=:\xi_{\epsilon,\eta}(\gamma)

Due to (
11
),
\xi_{\epsilon,\eta}(\gamma)\to 0
as
\gamma\to 0
.

Combining everything together in (
38
) we have

\displaystyle{\sf P}(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma})\geq 1-\delta-\eta-\xi_{\epsilon,\eta}(\gamma).

Since
(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)
and
\gamma\in(0,1)
were chosen arbitrarily, taking infimum over
(T,D)
and letting
\gamma,\delta\to 0
we obtain

\liminf_{\gamma,\delta\to 0}\inf_{(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)}{\sf P}\left(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}\right)\geq 1-\eta.

Finally, letting
\eta\to 0
we obtain (
37a
).

To show (
37b
), consider the probability distribution
{\sf Q}_{2}^{*}
which attains

\min\left\{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q}):{\sf Q}\in\cup_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}\right\}.

Thus, there exists
e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
such that
{\sf Q}_{2}^{*}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}
, i.e.,
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf Q}_{2}^{*})
. Then,

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}\right)

\displaystyle\geq{\sf P}\left(e\notin D,D\neq\emptyset,T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}\right)

\displaystyle={\sf P}\left(e\notin D,D\neq\emptyset\right)-{\sf P}\left(e\notin D,D\neq\emptyset,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}\right)

\displaystyle=1-{\sf P}\left(e\in D,D\neq\emptyset\right)-{\sf P}\left(e\notin D,D\neq\emptyset,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\gamma-{\sf P}\left(e\notin D,D\neq\emptyset,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}\right)

(39)

where the last inequality follows from the desired error control that
{\sf P}\left(D\setminus\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\neq\emptyset\right)\leq\gamma
. We can now decompose the third term as

{\sf P}\left(e\notin D,D\neq\emptyset,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}\right)\leq p_{1}+p_{2},

such that

\displaystyle p_{1}

\displaystyle:={\sf P}\left(e\notin D,D\neq\emptyset,\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{2}^{*})<\frac{\eta}{\delta}\right)\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle p_{2}

\displaystyle:={\sf P}\left(T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta},\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{2}^{*})\geq\frac{\eta}{\delta}\right),

where
\eta
is an arbitrary constant in
(0,1)
. For the first term, by a change of measure from
{\sf P}
to
{\sf Q}_{2}^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle p_{1}

\displaystyle={\sf E}_{{\sf Q}_{2}^{*}}\left[\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{2}^{*});e\notin D,D\neq\emptyset,\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{2}^{*})<\frac{\eta}{\delta}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta}{\delta}{\sf Q}_{2}^{*}\left(e\notin D,D\neq\emptyset\right)\leq\eta,

where the last inequality follows from the desired error control that
{\sf Q}_{2}^{*}\left(\mathcal{A}({\sf Q}_{2}^{*})\setminus D\neq\emptyset,D\neq\emptyset\right)\leq\delta
. For the second term, we can bound it above by the similar manner as shown previously

\displaystyle p_{2}\leq{\sf P}\left(\frac{1}{(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}}\max_{1\leq n\leq(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}}\log\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{2}^{*})\geq\frac{\log\eta}{(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}}+\frac{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{2}^{*})}{(1-\epsilon)}\right)=:\xi_{\epsilon,\eta}(\delta)

Due to (
11
),
\xi_{\epsilon,\eta}(\delta)\to 0
as
\delta\to 0
.

Combining everything together in (
39
) we have

\displaystyle{\sf P}(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta})\geq 1-\gamma-\eta-\xi_{\epsilon,\eta}(\delta).

Since
(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)
and
\delta\in(0,1)
were chosen arbitrarily, taking infimum over
(T,D)
and letting
\gamma,\delta\to 0
we obtain

\liminf_{\gamma,\delta\to 0}\inf_{(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)}{\sf P}\left(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}\right)\geq 1-\eta.

Finally, letting
\eta\to 0
we obtain (
37b
), which along with (
37a
) completes the first part of the proof.

For the second part, we first show that when
\Psi=\Psi_{m,m}
we have

\cup_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}=\cup_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}=\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\{{\sf P}\}.

Indeed, the above holds since for any
{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\{{\sf P}\}
, as
|\mathcal{A}({\sf P})|=|\mathcal{A}({\sf Q})|=m
, there exists
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
such that
e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf Q})
, and also there exists
e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
such that
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf Q})
, i.e., both

{\sf Q}\in\cup_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}\quad\text{and}\quad{\sf Q}\in\cup_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}

hold equivalently.

Combined with this and using the fact that, for any
(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)
,

{\sf P}\left(D\setminus\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\neq\emptyset\right)={\sf P}\left(\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\setminus D\neq\emptyset\right)={\sf P}\left(D\neq\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\right)\leq\gamma\wedge\delta
, one can follow the similar technique as above to complete the proof.
∎

Since both (
15
) and (
16
) hold, from Theorem
4.1
as
\gamma,\delta\to 0
, we have

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq{\sf E}_{\sf P}\left[T^{*}\right]\lesssim\max_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\gamma|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e})}\right\}\bigvee\max_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\delta|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e})}\right\}.

Thus, the result follows by using Lemma
D.1
. When
\Psi=\Psi_{m,m}
the result can be established in a similar fashion.
∎

D.2
Some important lemmas

Now we prove three important lemmas which will be useful in the proofs of Theorems
4.4
,
4.5
and
4.6
. The next lemma provides an asymptotic lower bound on the optimal performance corresponding to joint detection and isolation.

Lemma D.2
.

Suppose that (
11
) holds,
\emptyset\in\Psi
and let
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}
.

(i)

If
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
, then as
\alpha,\beta\to 0
while
\gamma
and
\delta
are either fixed or go to
0
, we have

\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)\right\}\;\gtrsim\;\max_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\left\{\frac{|\log\beta|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}\right)}\right\}.

(ii)

If
{\sf P}\notin\mathcal{H}_{0}
, then as
\alpha,\beta\to 0
while
\gamma
and
\delta
are either fixed or go to
0
, we have

\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)\right\}\;\gtrsim\;\frac{|\log\alpha|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0}\right)}.

(iii)

If
{\sf P}\notin\mathcal{H}_{0}
, then as
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\to 0
, we have

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)\right\}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\;\frac{|\log\alpha|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0})}\bigvee\max_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\gamma|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e})}\right\}\bigvee\max_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\delta|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e})}\right\}.

In order to prove (i) fix
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
and denote the quantity
\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)\right\}
by
N(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)
. Then we need to to show that as
\alpha,\beta\to 0
, while
\gamma
and
\delta
are either fixed or go to
0
,

N(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)\;\gtrsim\;\max_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\left\{\frac{|\log\beta|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}\right)}\right\}.

We set

L_{\beta}:=\max_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\left\{\frac{|\log\beta|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}\right)}\right\},\quad\beta\in(0,1).

By Markov’s inequality, for any stopping time
T
, and
\beta,\epsilon\in(0,1)
,

{\sf E}_{\sf P}[T]\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\beta}{\sf P}\left(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\beta}\right).

Thus, it suffices to show for every
\epsilon\in(0,1)
we have

\liminf_{\alpha,\beta\to 0}\inf_{(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)}{\sf P}\left(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\beta}\right)\geq 1,

(40)

since this will further imply that

\liminf_{\alpha,\beta\to 0}\;N(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)/L_{\beta}\geq(1-\epsilon),

and the desired result will follow by letting
\epsilon\to 0
. In order to prove (
40
), we start by fixing arbitrary
(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)
and
\beta,\epsilon\in(0,1)
. Consider the probability distribution
{\sf Q}^{*}
which attains

\min\{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q}):{\sf Q}\in\cup_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}\}.

Thus, there exists
e\in\mathcal{K}
such that
{\sf Q}^{*}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}
. i.e.,
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf Q}^{*})
, which means
{\sf Q}^{*}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}
.
Then,

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\geq{\sf P}\left(D=\emptyset,T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\beta}\right)

\displaystyle={\sf P}\left(D=\emptyset\right)-{\sf P}\left(D=\emptyset,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\beta}\right)

\displaystyle=1-{\sf P}\left(D\neq\emptyset\right)-{\sf P}\left(D=\emptyset,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\alpha-{\sf P}\left(D=\emptyset,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\beta}\right)

(41)

where the last inequality follows from the desired error control that
{\sf P}(D\neq\emptyset)\leq\alpha
. We can now decompose the third term as

{\sf P}\left(D=\emptyset,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\beta}\right)\leq p_{1}+p_{2},

such that

\displaystyle p_{1}

\displaystyle:={\sf P}\left(D=\emptyset,\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}^{*})<\frac{\eta}{\beta}\right)\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle p_{2}

\displaystyle:={\sf P}\left(T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\beta},\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}^{*})\geq\frac{\eta}{\beta}\right),

where
\eta
is an arbitrary constant in
(0,1)
. For the first term, by a change of measure from
{\sf P}
to
{\sf Q}^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle p_{1}

\displaystyle={\sf E}_{{\sf Q}^{*}}\left[\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}^{*});D=\emptyset,\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}^{*})<\frac{\eta}{\beta}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta}{\beta}{\sf Q}^{*}\left(D=\emptyset\right)\leq\eta,

where the last inequality follows from the error control that
{\sf Q}^{*}\left(D=\emptyset\right)\leq\beta
. For the second term, we can bound it above by

\displaystyle p_{2}

\displaystyle={\sf P}\left(T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\beta},\log\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}^{*})\geq\log\eta+|\log\beta|\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\sf P}\left(\max_{1\leq n\leq(1-\epsilon)L_{\beta}}\log\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf Q}^{*})\geq\log\eta+|\log\beta|\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\sf P}\left(\frac{1}{(1-\epsilon)L_{\beta}}\max_{1\leq n\leq(1-\epsilon)L_{\beta}}\log\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf Q}^{*})\geq\frac{\log\eta}{(1-\epsilon)L_{\beta}}+\frac{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q}^{*})}{(1-\epsilon)}\right)=:\xi_{\epsilon,\eta}(\beta)

Due to (
11
),
\xi_{\epsilon,\eta}(\beta)\to 0
as
\beta\to 0
.

Combining everything together in (
41
) we have

\displaystyle{\sf P}(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\beta})\geq 1-\alpha-\eta-\xi_{\epsilon,\eta}(\beta).

Since
(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)
and
\beta\in(0,1)
were chosen arbitrarily, taking infimum over
(T,D)
and letting
\alpha,\beta\to 0
we obtain

\liminf_{\alpha,\beta\to 0}\inf_{(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)}{\sf P}\left(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\beta}\right)\geq 1-\eta.

Finally, letting
\eta\to 0
we obtain (
40
), which completes the proof of (i).

Next, in order to prove (ii) and (iii), we fix any arbitrary
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}
and set

L_{\alpha}:=\frac{|\log\alpha|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0})},\quad L_{\gamma}:=\max_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\;\left\{\frac{|\log\gamma|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e})}\right\},\quad\text{and}\quad L_{\delta}:=\max_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\delta|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e})}\right\}.

In a similar manner, to prove the second and third part of the proof, we will show that for every
\epsilon\in(0,1)
we have

\displaystyle\liminf_{\alpha,\beta\to 0}\inf_{(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)}{\sf P}\left(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\alpha}\right)\geq 1,

(42a)

\displaystyle\liminf_{\beta,\gamma,\delta\to 0}\inf_{(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)}{\sf P}\left(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}\right)\geq 1,\quad\text{and}

(42b)

\displaystyle\liminf_{\beta,\gamma,\delta\to 0}\inf_{(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)}{\sf P}\left(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}\right)\geq 1

(42c)

In order to prove (
42
), we start by fixing arbitrary
(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)
and
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta,\epsilon\in(0,1)
.
To show (
42a
), consider the probability distribution
{\sf Q}_{0}^{*}
which attains

\min\{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q}):{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}\}.

Then,

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\geq{\sf P}\left(D\neq\emptyset,T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle={\sf P}\left(D\neq\emptyset\right)-{\sf P}\left(D\neq\emptyset,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle=1-{\sf P}\left(D=\emptyset\right)-{\sf P}\left(D\neq\emptyset,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\alpha}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\beta-{\sf P}\left(D\neq\emptyset,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\alpha}\right)

(43)

where the last inequality follows from the desired error control that
{\sf P}(D=\emptyset)\leq\beta
. We can now decompose the third term as

{\sf P}\left(D\neq\emptyset,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\alpha}\right)\leq p_{1}+p_{2},

such that

\displaystyle p_{1}

\displaystyle:={\sf P}\left(D\neq\emptyset,\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{0}^{*})<\frac{\eta}{\alpha}\right)\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle p_{2}

\displaystyle:={\sf P}\left(T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\alpha},\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{0}^{*})\geq\frac{\eta}{\alpha}\right),

where
\eta
is an arbitrary constant in
(0,1)
. For the first term, by a change of measure from
{\sf P}
to
{\sf Q}_{0}^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle p_{1}

\displaystyle={\sf E}_{{\sf Q}_{0}^{*}}\left[\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{0}^{*});D\neq\emptyset,\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{0}^{*})<\frac{\eta}{\alpha}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta}{\alpha}{\sf Q}_{0}^{*}\left(D\neq\emptyset\right)\leq\eta,

where the last inequality follows from the error control that
{\sf Q}_{0}^{*}\left(D\neq\emptyset\right)\leq\alpha
. For the second term, we can bound it above by the similar manner as shown previously

\displaystyle p_{2}\leq{\sf P}\left(\frac{1}{(1-\epsilon)L_{\alpha}}\max_{1\leq n\leq(1-\epsilon)L_{\alpha}}\log\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{0}^{*})\geq\frac{\log\eta}{(1-\epsilon)L_{\alpha}}+\frac{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{0}^{*})}{(1-\epsilon)}\right)=:\xi_{\epsilon,\eta}(\alpha)

Due to (
11
),
\xi_{\epsilon,\eta}(\alpha)\to 0
as
\alpha\to 0
.

Combining everything together in (
43
) we have

\displaystyle{\sf P}(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\alpha})\geq 1-\beta-\eta-\xi_{\epsilon,\eta}(\alpha).

Since
(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
were chosen arbitrarily, taking infimum over
(T,D)
and letting
\alpha,\beta\to 0
we obtain

\liminf_{\alpha,\beta\to 0}\inf_{(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)}{\sf P}\left(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\alpha}\right)\geq 1-\eta.

Finally, letting
\eta\to 0
we obtain (
42a
), which completes the proof of (ii).

To show (
42b
), consider the probability distribution
{\sf Q}_{1}
which attains

\min\left\{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q}):{\sf Q}\in\cup_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}\right\}.

Thus, there exists
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
such that
{\sf Q}_{1}^{*}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}
, i.e.,
e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf Q}_{1}^{*})
. Then,

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle\geq{\sf P}\left(e\in D,T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle={\sf P}\left(e\in D\right)-{\sf P}\left(e\in D,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle=1-{\sf P}\left(e\notin D\right)-{\sf P}\left(e\in D,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\beta-\delta-{\sf P}\left(e\in D,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}\right)

(44)

where the last inequality follows from the fact that

\{e\notin D\}\subseteq\{D=\emptyset\}\cup\{\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\setminus D\neq\emptyset,D\neq\emptyset\}

and the error controls that
{\sf P}(D=\emptyset)\leq\beta
and
{\sf P}\left(\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\setminus D\neq\emptyset,D\neq\emptyset\right)\leq\delta
. We can now decompose the third term as

{\sf P}\left(e\in D,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}\right)\leq p_{1}+p_{2},

such that

\displaystyle p_{1}

\displaystyle:={\sf P}\left(e\in D,\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{1}^{*})<\frac{\eta}{\gamma}\right)\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle p_{2}

\displaystyle:={\sf P}\left(T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma},\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{1}^{*})\geq\frac{\eta}{\gamma}\right),

where
\eta
is an arbitrary constant in
(0,1)
. For the first term, by a change of measure from
{\sf P}
to
{\sf Q}_{1}^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle p_{1}

\displaystyle={\sf E}_{{\sf Q}_{1}^{*}}\left[\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{1}^{*});e\in D,\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{1}^{*})<\frac{\eta}{\gamma}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta}{\gamma}{\sf Q}_{1}^{*}\left(e\in D\right)\leq\eta,

where the last inequality follows from the fact that

\{e\in D\}\subseteq\{D\setminus\mathcal{A}({\sf Q}_{1}^{*})\neq\emptyset\}

and the error control that
{\sf Q}_{1}^{*}\left(D\setminus\mathcal{A}({\sf Q}_{1}^{*})\neq\emptyset\right)\leq\gamma
. For the second term, we can bound it above by the similar manner as shown previously

\displaystyle p_{2}\leq{\sf P}\left(\frac{1}{(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}}\max_{1\leq n\leq(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}}\log\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{1}^{*})\geq\frac{\log\eta}{(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}}+\frac{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{1}^{*})}{(1-\epsilon)}\right)=:\xi_{\epsilon,\eta}(\gamma)

Due to (
11
),
\xi_{\epsilon,\eta}(\gamma)\to 0
as
\gamma\to 0
.

Combining everything together in (
44
) we have

\displaystyle{\sf P}(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma})\geq 1-\beta-\delta-\eta-\xi_{\epsilon,\eta}(\gamma).

Since
(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)
and
\gamma\in(0,1)
were chosen arbitrarily, taking infimum over
(T,D)
and letting
\beta,\gamma,\delta\to 0
we obtain

\liminf_{\beta,\gamma,\delta\to 0}\inf_{(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)}{\sf P}\left(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\gamma}\right)\geq 1-\eta.

Finally, letting
\eta\to 0
we obtain (
42b
).

To show (
42c
), consider the probability distribution
{\sf Q}_{2}^{*}
which attains

\min\left\{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q}):{\sf Q}\in\cup_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}\right\}.

Thus, there exists
e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
such that
{\sf Q}_{2}^{*}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}
, i.e.,
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf Q}_{2}^{*})
.Then,

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}\right)

\displaystyle\geq{\sf P}\left(e\notin D,D\neq\emptyset,T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}\right)

\displaystyle={\sf P}\left(e\notin D,D\neq\emptyset\right)-{\sf P}\left(e\notin D,D\neq\emptyset,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}\right)

\displaystyle=1-{\sf P}\left(\left(e\in D\right)\cup\left(D=\emptyset\right)\right)-{\sf P}\left(e\notin D,D\neq\emptyset,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}\right)

\displaystyle=1-{\sf P}\left(e\in D\right)-{\sf P}\left(D=\emptyset\right)-{\sf P}\left(e\notin D,D\neq\emptyset,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 1-\gamma-\beta-{\sf P}\left(e\notin D,D\neq\emptyset,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}\right)

(45)

where the third equality holds because the events
\{e\in D\}
and
\{D=\emptyset\}
are disjoint and the last inequality follows from the desired error controls that
{\sf P}\left(D\setminus\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\neq\emptyset\right)\leq\gamma
and
{\sf P}\left(D=\emptyset\right)\leq\beta
. We can now decompose the third term as

{\sf P}\left(e\notin D,D\neq\emptyset,T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}\right)\leq p_{1}+p_{2},

such that

\displaystyle p_{1}

\displaystyle:={\sf P}\left(e\notin D,D\neq\emptyset,\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{2}^{*})<\frac{\eta}{\delta}\right)\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle p_{2}

\displaystyle:={\sf P}\left(T<(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta},\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{2}^{*})\geq\frac{\eta}{\delta}\right),

where
\eta
is an arbitrary constant in
(0,1)
. For the first term, by a change of measure from
{\sf P}
to
{\sf Q}_{2}^{*}
, we have

\displaystyle p_{1}

\displaystyle={\sf E}_{{\sf Q}_{2}^{*}}\left[\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{2}^{*});e\notin D,D\neq\emptyset,\Lambda_{T}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{2}^{*})<\frac{\eta}{\delta}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta}{\delta}{\sf Q}_{2}^{*}\left(e\notin D,D\neq\emptyset\right)\leq\eta,

where the last inequality follows from the desired error control that
{\sf Q}_{2}^{*}\left(\mathcal{A}({\sf Q}_{2}^{*})\setminus D\neq\emptyset,D\neq\emptyset\right)\leq\delta
. For the second term, we can bound it above by the similar manner as shown previously

\displaystyle p_{2}\leq{\sf P}\left(\frac{1}{(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}}\max_{1\leq n\leq(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}}\log\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{2}^{*})\geq\frac{\log\eta}{(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}}+\frac{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{2}^{*})}{(1-\epsilon)}\right)=:\xi_{\epsilon,\eta}(\delta)

Due to (
11
),
\xi_{\epsilon,\eta}(\delta)\to 0
as
\delta\to 0
.

Combining everything together in (
39
) we have

\displaystyle{\sf P}(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta})\geq 1-\gamma-\beta-\eta-\xi_{\epsilon,\eta}(\delta).

Since
(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)
and
\delta\in(0,1)
were chosen arbitrarily, taking infimum over
(T,D)
and letting
\beta,\gamma,\delta\to 0
we obtain

\liminf_{\beta,\gamma,\delta\to 0}\inf_{(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)}{\sf P}\left(T\geq(1-\epsilon)L_{\delta}\right)\geq 1-\eta.

Finally, letting
\eta\to 0
we obtain (
42c
), which along with (
42b
) and (
42a
) completes the proof of (iii).
∎

The next lemma provides an asymptotic lower bound on the optimal performance corresponding to pure detection.

Lemma D.3
.

Suppose that (
11
) holds,
\emptyset\in\Psi
and let
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}
.

(i)

If
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
, then as
\alpha,\beta\to 0
, we have

\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{D}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta)\right\}\;\gtrsim\;\max_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\left\{\frac{|\log\beta|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}\right)}\right\}.

(ii)

If
{\sf P}\notin\mathcal{H}_{0}
, then as
\alpha,\beta\to 0
, we have

\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{D}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta)\right\}\;\gtrsim\;\frac{|\log\alpha|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0}\right)}.

Let
\gamma=\delta=1
. Then
\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)=\mathcal{D}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta)
, which leads to

\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{D}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta)\right\}=\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,1,1)\right\}.

Thus, the results follow by using Lemma
D.2
(i) and Lemma
D.2
(ii) respectively.
∎

The next lemma provides an asymptotic lower bound on the optimal performance when we are interested to control the FWER.

Lemma D.4
.

Suppose that (
11
) holds,
\emptyset\in\Psi
and let
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}
.

(i)

If
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
, then as
\gamma,\delta\to 0
, we have

\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)\right\}\;\gtrsim\;\max_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\left\{\frac{|\log\delta|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}\right)}\right\}.

(ii)

If
{\sf P}\notin\mathcal{H}_{0}
, then as
\gamma,\delta\to 0
, we have

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)\right\}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\;\max_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\gamma|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0})\wedge\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e})}\right\}\bigvee\max_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\delta|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e})}\right\}.

If
\alpha\geq\gamma
and
\beta\geq\delta
, then by (
3
), we have
\mathcal{E}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta)
. Furthermore, for any arbitrary
(T,D)\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)
and
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}
, we have from definition,

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(D\setminus\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\neq\emptyset\right)\leq\gamma,\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle{\sf P}\left(\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\setminus D\neq\emptyset,D\neq\emptyset\right)\leq{\sf P}\left(\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\setminus D\neq\emptyset\right)\leq\delta,

which implies
(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi\setminus\emptyset}(\gamma,\delta)
and thus,
\mathcal{E}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{\Psi\setminus\emptyset}(\gamma,\delta)
. This gives,

\mathcal{E}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta)\cap\mathcal{C}_{\Psi\setminus\emptyset}(\gamma,\delta)=\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)\right\}\leq\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)\right\}.

Now, let
\alpha=\gamma
and
\beta=\delta
, and the results follow immediately by using Lemma
D.2
(i) and Lemma
D.2
(iii) respectively.
∎

D.3
Proof of Theorem
4.4

Fix
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
. Since (
17
) holds, from Theorem
4.2
(i), as
\beta\to 0
, we have

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{D}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta)\right\}\leq{\sf E}_{\sf P}[T^{*}_{det}]\;\lesssim\;\max_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\left\{\frac{|\log\beta|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}\right)}\right\},

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)\right\}\leq{\sf E}_{\sf P}\left[T^{*}\right]\;\lesssim\;\max_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\left\{\frac{|\log\beta|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}\right)}\right\},

and as
\delta\to 0
we have

\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)\right\}\leq{\sf E}_{\sf P}\left[T^{*}_{fwer}\right]\;\lesssim\;\max_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\left\{\frac{|\log\delta|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}\right)}\right\}.

Thus, the results follow by using Lemma
D.3
(i), Lemma
D.2
(i) and Lemma
D.4
(i) respectively.
∎

D.4
Proof of Theorem
4.5

Fix
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}
. Since (
18
) holds, from Theorem
4.2
(ii), as
\alpha\to 0
we have

\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{D}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta)\right\}\leq{\sf E}_{\sf P}\left[T^{*}_{det}\right]\lesssim\frac{|\log\alpha|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0}\right)}.

Thus, the result follows by using Lemma
D.3
(ii).
Next, as
\alpha,\gamma,\delta\to 0
such that
|\log\alpha|>>|\log\gamma|\vee|\log\delta|
, again from Theorem
4.2
(ii), we have

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)\right\}\leq{\sf E}_{\sf P}\left[T^{*}\right]\lesssim\frac{|\log\alpha|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0}\right)},

and on the other hand, as
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\to 0
such that
|\log\alpha|>>|\log\gamma|\vee|\log\delta|
, by using Lemma
D.2
(iii) we have

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)\right\}\geq\frac{|\log\alpha|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0})},

which leads to the result.
Furthermore, when both (
15
) and (
18
) hold, as
\gamma,\delta\to 0
such that
|\log\gamma|>>|\log\delta|
, from Theorem
4.2
(ii), we have

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)\right\}\leq{\sf E}_{\sf P}\left[T^{*}_{fwer}\right]\;\lesssim\max_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\gamma|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0})\wedge\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e})}\right\},

and on the other hand, from Lemma
D.4
(ii), we have

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)\right\}\gtrsim\;\max_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\gamma|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0})\wedge\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e})}\right\},

which completes the proof.
∎

D.5
Proof of Theorem
4.6

Fix
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}
. Since (
15
) - (
16
) - (
18
) hold, from Theorem
4.2
(ii), as
\gamma,\delta\to 0
we have

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq{\sf E}_{\sf P}\left[T^{*}_{fwer}\right]\;\lesssim\max_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\gamma|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0})\wedge\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e})}\right\}\bigvee\max_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\delta|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}\right)}\right\},

and as
\alpha,\gamma,\delta\to 0
we have

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq{\sf E}_{\sf P}\left[T^{*}\right]\;\lesssim\;\frac{|\log\alpha|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0}\right)}\bigvee\max_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\gamma|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}\right)}\right\}\bigvee\max_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\delta|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}\right)}\right\}.

Thus, the results follow by using Lemma
D.4
(ii) and Lemma
D.2
(iii) respectively.
∎

D.6
Proof of Remark
4.3

We only prove for the case of Theorem
4.3
