since for the other case, it follows in a similar fashion. Without loss of generality, suppose that only (
15
) holds and
\gamma,\delta\to 0
such that
|\log\gamma|>>|\log\delta|
. Then
from Theorem
4.1
we have

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)\right\}\;\leq\;{\sf E}_{\sf P}\left[T^{*}\right]\;\lesssim\;\max_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\gamma|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e})}\right\},

and from Lemma
D.1
we have

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)\right\}\;\gtrsim\;\max_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\gamma|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e})}\right\},

which completes the proof.
∎

D.7
Proof of Remark
4.4

We only prove for the case of Theorem
4.5
since for the other case, it follows in a similar fashion. Without loss of generality, suppose that (
19
) is satisfied. Then as
\gamma,\delta\to 0
, from Theorem
4.2
(ii) we have

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)\right\}

\displaystyle\leq{\sf E}_{\sf P}\left[T^{*}_{fwer}\right]\;\lesssim\min_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\gamma|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0};s_{e}\right)}\right\}\bigvee\max_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\delta|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e};s^{\prime}_{e}\right)}\right\},

and from Lemma
D.4
(ii) we have

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{E}_{\Psi}(\gamma,\delta)\right\}\gtrsim\;\frac{|\log\gamma|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0})}\bigvee\max_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\delta|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e})}\right\}.

As
|\log\gamma|>>|\log\delta|
the second terms from both bounds vanish and since (
18
) holds, the result follows.
∎

The conditions (
15
)-(
16
)-(
17
)-(
18
) may still be satisfied even if
s_{e}
or
s^{\prime}_{e}
is a strict subset of
[K]
for every
e\in\mathcal{K}
. The following proposition provides sufficient conditions for this in the presence of some independence and is crucial in establishing results of asymptotic optimality in Appendix
F
and
G
.

Proposition D.1
.

Suppose that (
11
)-(
12
) hold and
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}
.

(i)

If
{\sf P}\notin\mathcal{H}_{0}
, (
15
) holds when
s=s^{\prime}_{\bar{e}}
is independent of its complement under
{\sf P}
and

\displaystyle{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0},

(46)

where
\bar{e}
and
{\sf Q}_{1}
are such that

\bar{e}\in\argmin_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\;\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e};s^{\prime}_{e}\right)\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\sf Q}_{1}\in\argmin_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,\bar{e}}}\;\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s^{\prime}_{\bar{e}}\right).

(ii)

If
{\sf P}\notin\mathcal{H}_{0}
, (
16
) holds when
s=s^{\prime}_{\bar{e}}
is independent of its complement under
{\sf P}
and

\displaystyle{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0},

(47)

where
\bar{e}
and
{\sf Q}_{1}
are such that

\bar{e}\in\argmin_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\;\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e};s^{\prime}_{e}\right)\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\sf Q}_{1}\in\argmin_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,\bar{e}}}\;\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s^{\prime}_{\bar{e}}\right).

(iii)

If
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
, (
17
) holds when
s=s_{\bar{e}}
is independent of its complement under
{\sf P}
and

\displaystyle{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0},

(48)

where
\bar{e}
and
{\sf Q}_{1}
are such that

\bar{e}\in\argmin_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\;\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e};s_{e}\right)\qquad\text{and}\qquad{\sf Q}_{1}\in\argmin_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,\bar{e}}}\;\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{\bar{e}}\right),

(iv)

If
{\sf P}\notin\mathcal{H}_{0}
, (
18
) holds when there exists
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
such that
s=s_{e}
is independent of its complement under
{\sf P}
and

\displaystyle{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\in\mathcal{H}_{0},\quad\text{where}\quad{\sf Q}_{1}\in\argmin_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s_{e}\right).

(49)

The proofs are as follows.

(i)

(
15
) holds because of the following observation that,

\displaystyle\min_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\right)=\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}},{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P}^{s},{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\right)=\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{1};s^{\prime}_{\bar{e}})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,\bar{e}};s^{\prime}_{\bar{e}})=\min_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e};s^{\prime}_{e})\leq\min_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}).

In the above, the first inequality holds because
\bar{e}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
and
{\sf Q}_{1}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,\bar{e}}
, which combined with (
46
), implies that

{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,\bar{e}}\subseteq\cup_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}.

The second equality follows from Lemma
B.3
under the assumptions (
11
)-(
12
), and the last inequality follows from Lemma
B.2
.

(ii)

(
16
) holds because of the following observation that,

\displaystyle\min_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\right)=\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}},{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P}^{s},{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\right)=\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{1};s^{\prime}_{\bar{e}})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,\bar{e}};s^{\prime}_{\bar{e}})=\min_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e};s^{\prime}_{e})\leq\min_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}).

In the above, the first inequality holds because
\bar{e}\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
and
{\sf Q}_{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,\bar{e}}
, which combined with (
47
), implies that

{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,\bar{e}}\subseteq\cup_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}.

The second equality follows from Lemma
B.3
under the assumptions (
11
)-(
12
), and the last inequality follows from Lemma
B.2
.

(iii)

(
17
) holds because of the following observation that,

\displaystyle\min_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\right)=\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}},{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P}^{s},{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\right)=\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{1};s_{\bar{e}})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,\bar{e}};s_{\bar{e}})=\min_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e};s_{e})\leq\min_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}).

In the above, the first inequality holds because
{\sf Q}_{1}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,\bar{e}}
, which combined with (
48
), implies that

{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,\bar{e}}\subseteq\cup_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}.

The second equality follows from Lemma
B.3
under the assumptions (
11
)-(
12
), and the last inequality follows from Lemma
B.2
.

(iv)

(
18
) holds because of the following observation that,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\right)=\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}},{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P}^{s},{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\right)=\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{1};s_{e})=\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0};s_{e})\leq\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0}).

In the above, the first inequality, and the last equality hold because of (
49
). The second equality follows from Lemma
B.3
under the assumptions (
11
)-(
12
) and the last inequality follows from Lemma
B.2
.

∎

E
Detection and isolation of anomalous sources: the case of independence

E.1
An important proposition

Proposition E.1
.

If for every
k\in[K]
,
{\sf P}^{k}\in\mathcal{G}^{k}
,
{\sf Q}^{k}\in\mathcal{H}^{k}
there are positive numbers

\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},{\sf Q}^{k})\quad\text{ and }\quad\mathcal{I}({\sf Q}^{k},{\sf P}^{k}),

such that as
n\to\infty
we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\sf P}^{k}\left(\max_{1\leq m\leq n}Z_{m}({\sf P}^{k},{\sf Q}^{k})\geq n\rho\right)\to 0\qquad\forall\,\rho>\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},{\sf Q}^{k}),\\
&{\sf Q}^{k}\left(\max_{1\leq m\leq n}Z_{m}({\sf Q}^{k},{\sf P}^{k})\geq n\rho\right)\to 0\qquad\forall\,\rho>\mathcal{I}({\sf Q}^{k},{\sf P}^{k}),\end{split}

(50)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}{\sf P}^{k}\left(Z_{n}({\sf P}^{k},{\sf Q}^{k})<n\rho\right)<\infty\qquad\forall\,\rho<\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},{\sf Q}^{k}),\\
&\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}{\sf Q}^{k}\left(Z_{n}({\sf Q}^{k},{\sf P}^{k})<n\rho\right)<\infty\qquad\forall\,\rho<\mathcal{I}({\sf Q}^{k},{\sf P}^{k}),\end{split}

(51)

then (
11
) and (
12
) hold, for any
s\subseteq[K]
, with

\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{s},{\sf Q}^{s})=\sum_{k\in s}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},{\sf Q}^{k})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathcal{I}({\sf Q}^{s},{\sf P}^{s})=\sum_{k\in s}\mathcal{I}({\sf Q}^{k},{\sf P}^{k}).

Fix
e\in\mathcal{K}
,
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}
,
{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}\cup\mathcal{H}_{0}
,
e\subseteq s\subseteq[K].

Since the sources belonging to
s
are independent both under
{\sf P}
and
{\sf Q}
, and (
50
)-(
51
) hold for every source
k\in[K]
, by an application of Lemma
B.3
, we observe that conditions (
11
)-(
12
) hold for
{\sf P}
,
{\sf Q}
and
s
.
Furthermore, we apply the same lemma once again to have

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{s},{\sf Q}^{s})=\sum_{k\in s}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},{\sf Q}^{k})\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathcal{I}({\sf Q}^{s},{\sf P}^{s})=\sum_{k\in s}\mathcal{I}({\sf Q}^{k},{\sf P}^{k}),

which completes the proof.
∎

In order to prove the next results we introduce some further notations.
For every
k\in[K]
, we order the set of likelihood ratio statistics excluding
\Lambda_{k}(n)
, i.e.,
\{\Lambda_{j}(n):j\neq k\}
and denote them by

\Lambda_{-k,(1)}(n)\geq\dots\geq\Lambda_{-k,(K-1)}(n),

along with the corresponding indices
i_{-k,1}(n),\dots,i_{-k,K-1}(n)
, i.e.,

\Lambda_{-k,(j)}(n)=\Lambda_{i_{-k,j}(n)}(n),\ \text{for every}\ j\in[K-1].

Furthermore, we denote by
p_{k}(n)
the number of above likelihood ratio statistics that are greater than 1. Formally,

\Lambda_{-k,(1)}(n)\geq\dots\geq\Lambda_{-k,(p_{k}(n))}>1\geq\Lambda_{-k,(p_{k}(n)+1)}\geq\dots\geq\Lambda_{-k,(K-1)}(n).

Then it is not difficult to observe that

p_{k}(n)=\begin{cases}p(n)-1\ &\text{if}\ k\in\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{p(n)}(n)\}\\
p(n)&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}.

(52)

Furthermore, when the prior information is
\Psi_{l,u}
, with
0\leq l\leq u\leq K
, we define the following two quantities for every
k\in[K]
:

m^{1}_{k}(n):=\text{median}\{(l-1)\vee 0,p_{k}(n),u-1\}\quad\text{and}\quad m^{0}_{k}(n):=\text{median}\{l\vee 1,p_{k}(n),u\}.

For the rest of this section we consider
\Psi=\Psi_{l,u}
, where
l\leq u
.

E.2
Some important lemmas

In this section we present some important lemmas which plays a crucial role to simplify the detection and isolation statistics in the proposed procedures so that one can obtain them in a more implementable version.

Lemma E.1
.

Suppose
0\leq l\leq u\leq K
. Then for every
k\in[K]
,
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and any arbitrary reference distribution
{\sf P}_{0}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
, we have

\frac{\max\left\{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf P}_{0}):{\sf P}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,k}\right\}}{\max\left\{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf P}_{0}):{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}\right\}}=\Lambda_{k}(n)\prod_{j=1}^{m^{1}_{k}(n)}\Lambda_{-e,(j)}(n),

(53)

and

\frac{\max\left\{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf P}_{0}):{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,k}\right\}}{\max\left\{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf P}_{0}):{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}\right\}}=\prod_{j=1}^{m^{0}_{k}(n)}\Lambda_{-e,(j)}(n).

(54)

We only prove (
53
) since (
54
) can be proved in a similar way. Fix any arbitrary reference distribution
{\sf P}_{0}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
. For every
j\in[K]
define

\Lambda^{1}_{j}(n):=\max\{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf P}_{0}^{j}):{\sf P}\in\mathcal{G}^{j}\}\quad\text{and}\quad\Lambda^{0}_{j}(n):=\max\{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf P}_{0}^{j}):{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}^{j}\}.

Note that, for every
j\in[K]
,

\Lambda_{j}(n)=\Lambda^{1}_{j}(n)/\Lambda^{0}_{j}(n).

(55)

Furthermore, for any
{\sf P}\neq{\sf P}_{0}
, we have the following decomposition due to independence across the streams

\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf P}_{0})=\prod_{j=1}^{K}\Lambda_{n}({\sf P}^{j},{\sf P}_{0}^{j})=\prod_{\{j\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\Lambda_{n}({\sf P}^{j},{\sf P}_{0}^{j})\prod_{\{j\}\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\Lambda_{n}({\sf P}^{j},{\sf P}_{0}^{j}).

(56)

Fix any arbitrary
k\in[K]
and a subset of units
A\in\Psi_{l,u}
such that
\{k\}\in A
. Then from (
60
) one can obtain that

\displaystyle\max\{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf P}_{0}):{\sf P}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,k},\;\;\mathcal{A}({\sf P})=A\}=\prod_{\{j\}\in A}\Lambda^{1}_{j}(n)\prod_{\{j\}\notin A}\Lambda^{0}_{j}(n),\;\;\text{and}

\displaystyle\max\{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf P}_{0}):{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}\}=\prod_{j=1}^{K}\Lambda_{j}^{0}(n).

Due to (
59
), the above two expressions lead to

\frac{\max\{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf P}_{0}):{\sf P}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,k},\;\;\mathcal{A}({\sf P})=A\}}{\max\{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf P}_{0}):{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}\}}=\prod_{\{j\}\in A}\Lambda_{j}(n).

Now we further maximize the above quantity over all possible
A\in\Psi_{l,u}
.
Since
\{k\}\in A
and
l\leq|A|\leq u
,
\argmax_{A\in\Psi_{l,u}}\prod_{\{j\}\in A}\Lambda_{j}(n)
consists of sources with indices

\displaystyle\{k,i_{-k,1}(n),\dots,i_{-k,p_{k}(n)}(n)\}\quad

\displaystyle\text{if}\;\;\;l-1\vee 0\leq p_{k}(n)\leq u-1,

\displaystyle\{k,i_{-k,1}(n),\dots,i_{-k,l-1}(n)\}\quad

\displaystyle\text{if}\;\;\;p_{k}(n)\leq l-1\vee 0,\quad\text{or}

\displaystyle\{k,i_{-k,1}(n),\dots,i_{-k,u-1}(n)\}\quad

\displaystyle\text{if}\;\;\;p_{k}(n)\geq u-1.

This complete the proof.
∎

In the next lemma, we provide a simplification for the maximum of the detection statistics when
l=0
and
u\leq K
.

Lemma E.2
.

Consider
0=l<u\leq K
and
s_{k}=[K]
for every
k\in[K]
. Then for every
k\in[K]
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\max_{k\in[K]}\;\Lambda_{k,\rm det}(n)=\prod_{j=1}^{m(n)}\Lambda_{(j)}(n),

(57)

where
m(n):=median\{1,p(n),u\}
. Furthermore, when
s_{k}=[K]
for every
k\in[K]
or
s_{k}=\{k\}
for every
k\in[K]
, then

\max_{k\in[K]}\;\Lambda_{k,\rm det}(n)>1\quad\text{if and only if}\quad p(n)\geq 1.

Let
s_{k}=[K]
for every
k\in[K]
. For proving (
57
), we only consider the case when
p(n)<u
since for the case when
p(n)\geq u
, it can be proved in a similar way. Now, in this case,

m(n)=\begin{cases}p(n)\;\;&\text{if}\;\;p(n)\geq 1\\
1\;\;&\text{if}\;\;p(n)=0\end{cases}.

When
p(n)\geq 1
, for every
k\in\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{p(n)}(n)\}
, we have
m_{k}^{1}(n)=p_{k}(n)=p(n)-1
, and therefore, from (
53
) in Lemma
E.1
,

\Lambda_{k,\rm det}(n)=\Lambda_{k}(n)\prod_{j=1}^{p(n)-1}\Lambda_{-e,(j)}(n)=\prod_{j=1}^{p(n)}\Lambda_{(j)}(n).

On the other hand, when
k\notin\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{p(n)}(n)\}
, we have
m_{k}^{1}(n)=p_{k}(n)=p(n)
, and therefore, again from (
53
) in Lemma
E.1
,

\Lambda_{k,\rm det}(n)=\Lambda_{k}(n)\prod_{j=1}^{p(n)}\Lambda_{-e,(j)}(n)=\Lambda_{k}(n)\prod_{j=1}^{p(n)}\Lambda_{(j)}(n)\leq\prod_{j=1}^{p(n)}\Lambda_{(j)}(n),

where the inequality follows from the fact that
\Lambda_{k}(n)\leq 1
. Thus,

\max_{k\in[K]}\;\Lambda_{k,\rm det}(n)=\prod_{j=1}^{p(n)}\Lambda_{(j)}(n),

When
p(n)=0
, we have
m_{k}^{1}(n)=p_{k}(n)=0
for every
k\in[K]
, and therefore, using (
53
) in Lemma
E.1
,

\Lambda_{k,\rm det}(n)=\Lambda_{k}(n).

This implies
\max_{k\in[K]}\;\Lambda_{k,\rm det}(n)=\Lambda_{(1)}(n),

and this completes the first part of the proof.

Now we prove the second part. Suppose that
s_{k}=[K]
for every
k\in[K]
. Then from (
57
) that if
p(n)\geq 1
, we have

\max_{k\in[K]}\;\Lambda_{k,\rm det}(n)=\prod_{j=1}^{p(n)\wedge u}\Lambda_{(j)}(n)>1,

where the inequality follows
since
\Lambda_{(j)}(n)>1
for every
j\in[p(n)\wedge u]
. On the other hand, if
p(n)=0
, we have
\Lambda_{(1)}(n)<1
and
m(n)=0
. Therefore,
from (
57
),

\max_{k\in[K]}\;\Lambda_{k,\rm det}(n)=\Lambda_{(1)}(n)<1.

Now suppose that
s_{k}=\{k\}
for every
k\in[K]
. Then
\Lambda_{k,\rm det}(n)=\Lambda_{k}(n)
for every
k\in[K]
, and thus,

\max_{k\in[K]}\;\Lambda_{k,\rm det}(n)=\Lambda_{(1)}(n),

which is bigger than
1
if and only if
p(n)\geq 1
.
The proof is complete.
∎

In the next lemmas, we provide simplification for the isolation statistics depending on the values of
l
,
u
and
p(n)
.

Lemma E.3
.

Consider
l\geq 0
and
s^{\prime}_{k}=[K]
for every
k\in[K]
. Then we have
p(n)<l\vee 1
if and only if for every
k\in[K]
,

\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)=\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{-k,(l\vee 1)}(n)},

and in this case
D_{\rm iso}(n)
consists of sources with indices
\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{l\vee 1}(n)\}
.

From (
52
), we have
p(n)\leq l-1
if and only if
p_{k}(n)\leq l-1
for every
k\in[K]
. Equivalently, for every
k\in[K]
,
m_{k}^{1}(n)=(l-1)\vee 0
,
m_{k}^{0}(n)=l\vee 1
and therefore, dividing (
53
) by (
54
) in Lemma
E.1
, we have

\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)=\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)\prod_{j=1}^{(l-1)\vee 0}\Lambda_{-k,(j)}(n)}{\prod_{j=1}^{l\vee 1}\Lambda_{-k,(j)}(n)}=\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{-k,(l\vee 1)}(n)}.

Therefore,
\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)>1
if and only if
k\in\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{l\vee 1}(n)\}
.
∎

Lemma E.4
.

Consider
l\geq 0
and
s^{\prime}_{k}=[K]
for every
k\in[K]
. Then we have
p(n)=l\vee 1
if and only if

\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)=\begin{cases}\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{((l+1)\vee 2)}(n)}\ &\text{if}\ k\in\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{l\vee 1}(n)\}\\
\Lambda_{k}(n)\ &\text{otherwise}\end{cases},

and in this case
D_{\rm iso}(n)
consists of sources with indices
\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{l\vee 1}(n)\}
.

From (
52
), we have

\displaystyle p(n)=l\vee 1\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad p_{k}(n)=\begin{cases}(l-1)\vee 0\quad&\text{if}\quad k\in\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{l\vee 1}(n)\},\\
l\vee 1\quad\quad\;\;&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Equivalently, if
k\in\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{l\vee 1}(n)\}
,
m_{k}^{1}(n)=(l-1)\vee 0
,
m_{k}^{0}(n)=l\vee 1
, and dividing (
53
) by (
54
) in Lemma
E.1
gives that

\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)=\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)\prod_{j=1}^{(l-1)\vee 0}\Lambda_{-k,(j)}(n)}{\prod_{j=1}^{l\vee 1}\Lambda_{-k,(j)}(n)}=\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{-k,(l\vee 1)}(n)}=\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{((l+1)\vee 2)}(n)}>1,

otherwise,
m_{k}^{1}(n)=m_{k}^{0}(n)=l\vee 1
, and

\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)=\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)\prod_{j=1}^{l\vee 1}\Lambda_{-k,(j)}(n)}{\prod_{j=1}^{l\vee 1}\Lambda_{-k,(j)}(n)}=\Lambda_{k}(n)\leq 1.

∎

Lemma E.5
.

Consider
0\leq l<u\leq K
and
s^{\prime}_{k}=[K]
for every
k\in[K]
. Then
we have
l\vee 1<p(n)<u
if and only if
\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)=\Lambda_{k}(n)
for every
k\in[K]
and in this case
D_{\rm iso}(n)
consists of sources with indices
\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{p(n)}(n)\}
.

From (
52
), we have

\displaystyle(l+1)\vee 2\leq p(n)\leq u-1\;\;

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\;\;l\vee 1\leq p_{k}(n)\leq u-1\;\;\text{for every $k\in[K]$},

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow\;\;m_{k}^{1}(n)=m_{k}^{0}(n)=p_{k}(n)\;\;\text{for every $k\in[K]$}.

Equivalently, for every
k\in[K]
, dividing (
53
) by (
54
) in Lemma
E.1
gives that,

\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)=\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)\prod_{j=1}^{p_{k}(n)}\Lambda_{-k,(j)}(n)}{\prod_{j=1}^{p_{k}(n)}\Lambda_{-k,(j)}(n)}=\Lambda_{k}(n),

which also implies
D_{\rm iso}(n)
consists of sources with indices
\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{p(n)}(n)\}
.
∎

Lemma E.6
.

Consider
u\leq K
and
s^{\prime}_{k}=[K]
for every
k\in[K]
. Then we have

p(n)=u
if and only if

\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)=\begin{cases}\Lambda_{k}(n)\ &\text{if}\ k\in\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{u}(n)\}\\
\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{(u)}(n)}\ &\text{otherwise}\end{cases},

and in this case
D_{\rm iso}(n)
consists of sources with indices
\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{u}(n)\}
.

From (
52
), we have

\displaystyle p(n)=u\;\;\Leftrightarrow\;\;p_{k}(n)=\begin{cases}u-1\quad&\text{if}\quad k\in\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{u}(n)\}\\
u\quad\quad\;\;&\text{otherwise}\end{cases},

Equivalently, if
k\in\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{u}(n)\}
,
m_{k}^{1}(n)=m_{k}^{0}(n)=u-1
and dividing (
53
) by (
54
) in Lemma
E.1
gives that,

\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)=\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)\prod_{j=1}^{u-1}\Lambda_{-k,(j)}(n)}{\prod_{j=1}^{u-1}\Lambda_{-k,(j)}(n)}=\Lambda_{k}(n)>1,

otherwise,
m_{k}^{1}(n)=u-1
,
m_{k}^{0}(n)=u
, and

\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)=\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)\prod_{j=1}^{u-1}\Lambda_{-k,(j)}(n)}{\prod_{j=1}^{u}\Lambda_{-k,(j)}(n)}=\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{-k,(u)}(n)}=\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{(u)}(n)}\leq 1.

∎

Lemma E.7
.

Consider
u<K
and
s^{\prime}_{k}=[K]
for every
k\in[K]
.
Then we have
p(n)>u
if and only if for every
k\in[K]
,

\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)=\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{-k,(u)}(n)},

and in this case
D_{\rm iso}(n)
consists of sources with indices
\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{u}(n)\}
.

From (
52
), we have
p(n)\geq u+1\;\;\Leftrightarrow\;\;p_{k}(n)\geq u
for every
k\in[K]
. Equivalently, for every
k\in[K]
,
m_{k}^{1}(n)=u-1
,
m_{k}^{0}(n)=u
, and dividing (
53
) by (
54
) in Lemma
E.1
gives that,

\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)=\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)\prod_{j=1}^{u-1}\Lambda_{-k,(j)}(n)}{\prod_{j=1}^{u}\Lambda_{-k,(j)}(n)}=\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{-k,(u)}(n)}.

Therefore,
\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)>1
if and only if
k\in\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{u}(n)\}
. ∎

Lemma E.8
.

Consider
0<l=u<K
and
s^{\prime}_{k}=[K]
for every
k\in[K]
.
Then we have for every
k\in[K]
,

\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)=\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{-k,(u)}(n)},

and in this case,
D_{\rm iso}(n)
consists of sources with indices
\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{u}(n)\}
.

For every
k\in[K]
, we have
m_{k}^{1}(n)=u-1
,
m_{k}^{0}(n)=u
, and therefore, dividing (
53
) by (
54
) in Lemma
E.1
gives that

\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)=\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)\prod_{j=1}^{u-1}\Lambda_{-k,(j)}(n)}{\prod_{j=1}^{u}\Lambda_{-k,(j)}(n)}=\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{-k,(u)}(n)},

which is greater that
1
if and only if
k\in\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{u}(n)\}
. ∎

E.3
Proof of Theorem
5.1

Fix
(T,D)
to be either
\chi^{*}
or
\chi^{*}_{fwer}
.
First consider the case when
l=0
. Then by Lemma
E.2
if
p(T)=0
, then

\max_{k\in[K]}\;\Lambda_{k,\rm det}(T)<1,

which further implies that the test stops by
T_{0}
, i.e.,
T=T_{0}
. Thus, by definition
D=\emptyset
.

Next, we consider the general case when
l\geq 0
and
p(T)\geq 0
. Then by Lemmas
E.3
,
E.4
,
E.5
,
E.6
,
E.7
, we have

D(T)=\begin{cases}\{i_{1}(T),\dots,i_{l\vee 1}(T)\}\quad&\text{if}\;\;p(T)\leq l\vee 1\\
\{i_{1}(T),\dots,i_{p(T)}(T)\}\quad&\text{if}\;\;l\vee 1<p(T)<u\\
\{i_{1}(T),\dots,i_{u}(T)\}\quad&\text{if}\;\;u\leq p(T)\end{cases}.

This completes the proof.
∎

E.4
Proof of Theorem
5.2

We have
l\geq 1
and
s^{\prime}_{k}=[K]
for every
k\in[K]
.

(i)

By using Lemma
E.8
and the fact that
A_{k}=A
and
B_{k}=B
for every
k\in[K]
, one can write the stopping time
T^{*}
as

\displaystyle T^{*}

\displaystyle=\inf\bigg\{n\geq 1:\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{-k,(u)}(n)}\notin\left(\frac{1}{A},B\right)\;\;\text{for every}\,k\in[K]\quad\text{and}\quad|D_{\rm iso}(n)|=u\bigg\}.

Now note that,

\Lambda_{-k,(u)}(n)=\begin{cases}\Lambda_{(u+1)}(n)\ &\text{if}\ k\in\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{u}(n)\},\\
\Lambda_{(u)}(n)\ &\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Therefore,

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{-k,(u)}(n)}\notin\left(\frac{1}{A},B\right)\;\;\text{for every}\,k\in[K]\quad\text{and}\quad|D_{\rm iso}(n)|=u\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg\{\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{(u+1)}(n)}\geq B\;\;\text{for every}\ k\in\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{u}(n)\}

\displaystyle\qquad\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{(u)}(n)}\leq\frac{1}{A}\;\;\text{for every}\ k\notin\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{u}(n)\}\quad\text{and}\quad|D_{\rm iso}(n)|=u\bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\frac{\Lambda_{(u)}(n)}{\Lambda_{(u+1)}(n)}\geq\max\{A,B\}\right\}.

Note that, the condition
|D_{\rm iso}(n)|=u
disappears from the second equality by another application of Lemma
E.8
. The proof is complete.

(ii)

Since
A_{k}=A
and
B_{k}=B
for every
k\in[K]
, one can write the stopping time
T^{*}
as

\displaystyle T^{*}=\inf\left\{n\geq 1:\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)\notin\left(\frac{1}{A},B\right)\;\;\text{for every}\ k\in[K]\;\;\text{and}\;\;l\leq|D_{\rm iso}(n)|\leq u\right\}.

Now from Lemmas
E.3
,
E.4
,
E.5
,
E.6
and
E.7
, one can observe that at any time
n
and for any
u\leq K
, the condition
l\leq|D_{\rm iso}(n)|\leq u
is always satisfied irrespective of the value of
p(n)
. Hence, we omit this condition from the stopping event of
T^{*}
.
Next, let us consider the case when
u<K
and in this case, we will show that
T^{*}=\min\{T_{1},T_{2},T_{4},T_{5},T_{6}\}
. In order to do so,
we further intersect the stopping event of
T^{*}
with the disjoint decomposition of these following events:

\{p(n)<l\}\cup\{p(n)=l\}\cup\{l<p(n)<u\}\cup\{p(n)=u\}\cup\{p(n)>u\}.

Now, for the first event, i.e., when
p(n)<l
, by Lemma
E.3
, we have

\displaystyle\left\{p(n)<l\ \text{and}\ \Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)\notin\left(\frac{1}{A},B\right)\quad\text{for every}\ k\in[K]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg\{p(n)<l,\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{(l+1)}(n)}\geq B\quad\text{for every}\ k\in\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{l}(n)\}

\displaystyle\qquad\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{(l)}(n)}\leq\frac{1}{A}\quad\text{for every}\ k\notin\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{l}(n)\}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{p(n)<l\ \text{and}\ \frac{\Lambda_{(l)}(n)}{\Lambda_{(l+1)}(n)}\geq\max\{A,B\}\right\},

which is the stopping event of
T_{1}
.
For the second event, i.e., when
p(n)=l
, by Lemma
E.4
, we have

\displaystyle\left\{p(n)=l\ \text{and}\ \Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)\notin\left(\frac{1}{A},B\right)\quad\text{for every}\ k\in[K]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg\{p(n)=l,\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{(l+1)}(n)}\geq B\quad\text{for every}\ k\in\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{l}(n)\}

\displaystyle\qquad\Lambda_{k}(n)\leq\frac{1}{A}\quad\text{for every}\ k\notin\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{l}(n)\}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{p(n)=l,\frac{\Lambda_{(l)}(n)}{\Lambda_{(l+1)}(n)}\geq B\;\;\text{and}\;\;\Lambda_{(l+1)}(n)\leq\frac{1}{A}\right\},

which is the stopping event of
T_{2}
.
For the third even, i.e., when
l<p(n)<u
, by Lemma
E.5
, we have

\displaystyle\left\{l<p(n)<u\ \text{and}\ \Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)\notin\left(\frac{1}{A},B\right)\quad\text{for every}\ k\in[K]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{l<p(n)<u\ \text{and}\ \Lambda_{k}(n)\notin\left(\frac{1}{A},B\right)\quad\text{for every}\ k\in[K]\right\},

which is the stopping event of
T_{4}
.
For the fourth event, i.e., when
p(n)=u
, by Lemma
E.6
, we have

\displaystyle\left\{p(n)=u\quad\text{and}\quad\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)\notin\left(\frac{1}{A},B\right)\quad\text{for every}\ k\in[K]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg\{p(n)=u,\quad\Lambda_{k}(n)\geq B\quad\text{for every}\ k\in\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{u}(n)\}

\displaystyle\qquad\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{(u)}(n)}\leq\frac{1}{A}\quad\text{for every}\ k\notin\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{u}(n)\bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{p(n)=u,\quad\Lambda_{(u)}(n)\geq B\quad\text{and}\ \frac{\Lambda_{(u+1)}(n)}{\Lambda_{(u)}(n)}\leq\frac{1}{A}\right\},

which is the stopping event of
T_{5}
.
For the fifth event, i.e. when
p(n)>u
, by Lemma
E.7
, we have

\displaystyle\left\{p(n)>u\ \text{and}\ \Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)\notin\left(\frac{1}{A},B\right)\quad\text{for every}\ k\in[K]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg\{p(n)>u,\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{(u+1)}(n)}\geq B\quad\text{for every}\ k\in\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{u}(n)\}

\displaystyle\qquad\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{(u)}(n)}\leq\frac{1}{A}\quad\text{for every}\ k\notin\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{u}(n)\}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{p(n)>u\ \text{and}\ \frac{\Lambda_{(u)}(n)}{\Lambda_{(u+1)}(n)}\geq\max\{A,B\}\right\},

which is the stopping event of
T_{6}
.
Hence, the result follows.

Next consider the case when
u=K
. We similarly intersect the stopping event of
T^{*}
with the disjoint decomposition of these following events:

\{p(n)<l\}\cup\{p(n)=l\}\cup\{l<p(n)\leq K\}.

For the third event in above, i.e., when
l<p(n)\leq K
, by Lemma
E.5
and
E.6
, we have

\displaystyle\left\{l<p(n)\leq K,\;\;\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)\notin\left(\frac{1}{A},B\right)\;\;\text{for every}\ k\in[K]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{l<p(n)\leq K,\;\;\Lambda_{k}(n)\notin\left(\frac{1}{A},B\right)\;\;\text{for every}\ k\in[K]\right\},

which is the stopping event of
T_{3}
. Therefore, in this case,
T^{*}=\min\{T_{1},T_{2},T_{3}\}
. This completes the proof.

∎

E.5
Proof of Theorem
5.3

Since
C_{k}=C
and
D_{k}=D
for every
k\in[K]
, one can write the stopping times
T_{0}
and
T_{det}
as

\displaystyle T_{0}

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\max_{k\in[K]}\;\Lambda_{k,\rm det}(n)\leq 1/C\right\},

\displaystyle T_{det}

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\max_{k\in[K]}\;\Lambda_{k,\rm det}(n)\geq D\right\}.

Since
C,D>1
, stopping happens by
T_{0}
(resp.
T_{det}
) only if
\max_{k\in[K]}\;\Lambda_{k,\rm det}(n)\leq 1
(resp.
\max_{k\in[K]}\;\Lambda_{k,\rm det}(n)>1
), and by Lemma
E.2
, this happens only if
p(n)=0
(resp.
p(n)\geq 1
). Therefore, the stopping events in
T_{0}
and
T_{det}
remain the same even if they are intersected with
p(n)=0
and
p(n)\geq 1
respectively.
Thus,

\displaystyle T_{0}

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\max_{k\in[K]}\;\Lambda_{k,\rm det}(n)\leq 1/C,\;\;p(n)=0\right\}

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\Lambda_{(1)}(n)\leq 1/C\right\},

where the last equality follows from Lemma
E.2
. Similarly, we have

\displaystyle T_{det}

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\max_{k\in[K]}\;\Lambda_{k,\rm det}(n)\geq D,\;\;p(n)\geq 1\right\}

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\prod_{i=1}^{p(n)\wedge u}\Lambda_{(i)}(n)\geq D\right\},

where the last equality again follows from Lemma
E.2
, and this completes the first part.

Next we consider joint detection and isolation. Since
A_{k}=A
,
B_{k}=B
,
C_{k}=C
and
D_{k}=D
for every
k\in[K]
, one can write the stopping times
T_{joint}
as

\displaystyle T_{joint}

\displaystyle=\inf\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\;\max_{k\in[K]}\;\Lambda_{k,\rm det}(n)\geq D,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\;\;\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)\notin\left(1/A,B\right)\;\;\text{for every}\,k\in[K]\quad\text{and}\quad 1\leq|D_{\rm iso}(n)|\leq u\}.

Since
D>1
, stopping happens by
T_{joint}
only if
\max_{k\in[K]}\;\Lambda_{k,\rm det}(n)>1
, and by Lemma
E.2
, this happens only if
p(n)\geq 1
. Therefore, the stopping event in
T_{joint}
remains the same even if it is intersected with
p(n)\geq 1
.
Thus,

\displaystyle T_{joint}

\displaystyle=\inf\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\;\max_{k\in[K]}\;\Lambda_{k,\rm det}(n)\geq D,\;\;p(n)\geq 1,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\;\;\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)\notin\left(1/A,B\right)\;\;\text{for every}\,k\in[K]\quad\text{and}\quad 1\leq|D_{\rm iso}(n)|\leq u\},

\displaystyle=\inf\bigg\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\prod_{i=1}^{p(n)\wedge u}\Lambda_{(i)}(n)\geq D,\;\;p(n)\geq 1,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\;\;\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)\notin\left(1/A,B\right)\;\;\text{for every}\,k\in[K]\quad\text{and}\quad 1\leq|D_{\rm iso}(n)|\leq u\bigg\},

where the last equality again follows from Lemma
E.2
.

Now from Lemmas
E.4
,
E.5
,
E.6
and
E.7
, one can observe that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
u\leq K
,
p(n)\geq 1
implies
1\leq|D_{\rm iso}(n)|\leq u
. Hence, we omit this condition from the stopping event of
T_{joint}
.
First, let us consider the case when
u<K
and in this case, we will show that
T_{joint}=\min\{T_{1},T_{3},T_{4},T_{5}\}
. In order to do so,
we further intersect the stopping event of
T_{joint}
with the disjoint decomposition of
\{p(n)\geq 1\}
into these following events:

\{p(n)\geq 1\}=\{p(n)=1\}\cup\{1<p(n)<u\}\cup\{p(n)=u\}\cup\{p(n)>u\}.

Now, for the first event, i.e., when
p(n)=1
, by Lemma
E.4
, we have

\displaystyle\left\{\prod_{i=1}^{p(n)\wedge u}\Lambda_{(i)}(n)\geq D,\;\;p(n)=1,\;\;\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)\notin\left(1/A,B\right)\;\;\text{for every}\,k\in[K]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\Lambda_{(1)}(n)\geq D,\;\;\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{(2)}(n)}\geq B\;\;\text{for}\;\;k=i_{1}(n),\;\;\Lambda_{k}(n)\leq\frac{1}{A}\;\;\text{for every}\ k\neq i_{1}(n)\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\Lambda_{(1)}(n)\geq D,\;\;\frac{\Lambda_{(1)}(n)}{\Lambda_{(2)}(n)}\geq B,\;\;\Lambda_{(2)}(n)\leq\frac{1}{A}\right\},

which is the stopping event of
T_{1}
.
For the second event, i.e., when
1<p(n)<u
, by Lemma
E.5
, we have

\displaystyle\left\{\prod_{i=1}^{p(n)\wedge u}\Lambda_{(i)}(n)\geq D,\;\;1<p(n)<u,\;\;\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)\notin\left(\frac{1}{A},B\right)\;\;\text{for every}\ k\in[K]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\prod_{i=1}^{p(n)}\Lambda_{(i)}(n)\geq D,\;\;1<p(n)<u,\;\;\Lambda_{k}(n)\notin\left(\frac{1}{A},B\right)\;\;\text{for every}\ k\in[K]\right\},

which is the stopping event of
T_{3}
.
For the third event, i.e., when
p(n)=u
, by Lemma
E.6
, we have

\displaystyle\left\{\prod_{i=1}^{p(n)\wedge u}\Lambda_{(i)}(n)\geq D,\;\;p(n)=u,\;\;\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)\notin\left(\frac{1}{A},B\right)\;\;\text{for every}\ k\in[K]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Bigg\{\prod_{i=1}^{u}\Lambda_{(i)}(n)\geq D,\;\;p(n)=u,\;\;\Lambda_{k}(n)\geq B\;\;\text{for every}\ k\in\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{u}(n)\},

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{(u)}(n)}\leq\frac{1}{A}\;\;\text{for every}\ k\notin\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{u}(n)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\prod_{i=1}^{u}\Lambda_{(i)}(n)\geq D,\;\;p(n)=u,\;\;\Lambda_{(u)}(n)\geq B\;\;\text{and}\;\;\frac{\Lambda_{(u+1)}(n)}{\Lambda_{(u)}(n)}\leq\frac{1}{A}\right\},

which is the stopping event of
T_{4}
.
For the fourth event, i.e., when
p(n)>u
by Lemma
E.7
, we have

\displaystyle\left\{\prod_{i=1}^{p(n)\wedge u}\Lambda_{(i)}(n)\geq D,\;\;p(n)>u,\;\;\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)\notin\left(\frac{1}{A},B\right)\;\;\text{for every}\ k\in[K]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Bigg\{\prod_{i=1}^{u}\Lambda_{(i)}(n)\geq D,\;\;p(n)>u,\;\;\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{(u+1)}(n)}\geq B\;\;\text{for every}\ k\in\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{u}(n)\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\frac{\Lambda_{k}(n)}{\Lambda_{(u)}(n)}\leq\frac{1}{A}\;\;\text{for every}\ k\notin\{i_{1}(n),\dots,i_{u}(n)\}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\prod_{i=1}^{u}\Lambda_{(i)}(n)\geq D,\;\;p(n)>u,\;\;\frac{\Lambda_{(u)}(n)}{\Lambda_{(u+1)}(n)}\geq\max\{A,B\}\right\},

which is the stopping event of
T_{5}
. Hence, the result follows.

Next we consider the case when
u=K
, and similarly intersect the stopping event of
T_{joint}
with the disjoint decomposition of
\{p(n)\geq 1\}
into these following events:

\{p(n)\geq 1\}=\{p(n)=1\}\cup\{1<p(n)\leq K\}.

For the second event in above, i.e., when
1<p(n)\leq K
, by Lemma
E.5
and
E.6
, we have

\displaystyle\left\{\prod_{i=1}^{p(n)\wedge K}\Lambda_{(i)}(n)\geq D,\;\;1<p(n)\leq K,\;\;\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)\notin\left(\frac{1}{A},B\right)\;\;\text{for every}\ k\in[K]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\prod_{i=1}^{p(n)}\Lambda_{(i)}(n)\geq D,\;\;1<p(n)\leq K,\;\;\Lambda_{k}(n)\notin\left(\frac{1}{A},B\right)\;\;\text{for every}\ k\in[K]\right\},

which is the stopping event of
T_{2}
. Therefore, in this case
T_{joint}=\min\{T_{1},T_{2}\}
.
This completes the proof.
∎

E.6
Some important lemmas

Consider
l=0
, i.e.,
\Psi=\Psi_{0,u}
.
From Proposition
E.1
, for any
{\sf P},{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}
, we have

\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q})=\sum_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},{\sf Q}^{k})+\sum_{\{k\}\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},{\sf Q}^{k}).

(58)

Based on the above observation, we now present two important lemmas which will be useful to establish Theorem
5.4
.

Lemma E.9
.

For any distribution
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}
,

\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0})=\sum_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{H}^{k}).

Due to (
58
) it is not difficult to observe that the minimum of
\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q})
over
{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
is obtained by
{\sf Q}_{*}
that satisfies the following: for any
k\in[K]

{\sf Q}_{*}^{k}=\begin{cases}{\sf P}^{k}\quad&\text{if}\;\;\{k\}\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\\
\argmin_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}^{k}}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P}^{k},{\sf Q}\right)&\text{otherwise}\end{cases},

which yields

\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0})=\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{*})=\sum_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},{\sf Q}_{*}^{k})+\sum_{\{k\}\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},{\sf Q}_{*}^{k})=\sum_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{H}^{k}).

∎

Lemma E.10
.

For any distribution
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}
,

\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,k})=\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{H}^{k})+\mathbbm{1}_{\{|\mathcal{A}({\sf P})|=1\}}\min_{\{k\}\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{G}^{k}),

where
\mathbbm{1}_{A}
is the indicator of
A
, i.e., it takes the value
1
if
A
is true, or
0
, otherwise.

Since we want to minimize
\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q})
over all
{\sf Q}
such that there exists
\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
for which
{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,k}
. i.e., all
{\sf Q}
such that
\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\setminus\mathcal{A}({\sf Q})\neq\emptyset
and
|\mathcal{A}({\sf Q})|\geq 1
. First, consider the case when
|\mathcal{A}({\sf P})|=1
and let
\mathcal{A}({\sf P})=\{\{k_{1}\}\}
for some
k_{1}\in[K]
. Then due to (
58
) we must restrict our attention to all possible
{\sf Q}
such that
\{k_{1}\}\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf Q})
and
|\mathcal{A}({\sf Q})|=1
. Fix some
k_{2}\in\argmin_{k\neq k_{1}}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{G}^{k}\right)
. Then, the minimum is obtained by
{\sf Q}_{*}
that satisfies the following: for any
k\in[K]

{\sf Q}_{*}^{k}=\begin{cases}\argmin_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}^{k}}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P}^{k},{\sf Q}\right)\quad&\text{if}\;\;k=k_{1}\\
\argmin_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}^{k}}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P}^{k},{\sf Q}\right)&\text{if}\;\;k=k_{2}\\
{\sf P}^{k}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases},

which yields

\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,k})=\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{*})=\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k_{1}},\mathcal{H}^{k_{1}})+\min_{k\neq k_{1}}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{G}^{k}).

Next, consider the case when
|\mathcal{A}({\sf P})|>1
. Then due to (
58
) we must restrict our attention to all possible
{\sf Q}
such that its distribution differs from
{\sf P}
in exactly one source, which is a signal under
{\sf P}
and a non-signal under
{\sf Q}
. Fix some
k_{1}\in\argmin_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{H}^{k})
Then, the minimum is obtained by
{\sf Q}_{*}
that satisfies the following: for any
k\in[K]

{\sf Q}_{*}^{k}=\begin{cases}\argmin_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}^{k}}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P}^{k},{\sf Q}\right)&\text{if}\;\;k=k_{1}\\
{\sf P}^{k}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases},

which yields

\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,k})=\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q}_{*})=\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{H}^{k}).

The proof is complete.
∎

E.7
Proof of Theorem
5.4

We have
l=0
and
s_{k}=\{k\}
for every
k\in[K]
.

(i)

Since
u=K
,
s_{k}=s^{\prime}_{k}=\{k\}
, and
A_{k}=A
and
B_{k}=B
for every
k\in[K]
, we have

\displaystyle T^{*}_{fwer}

\displaystyle=\inf\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\;\Lambda_{k}(n)\notin\left(1/A,B\right)\;\;\text{for every}\,k\in[K]\;\;\text{and}\;\;D_{\rm iso}(n)\in\Psi_{0,K}\},

As
\Psi_{0,K}
is the powerset of
[K]
, we can omit the condition
\{D_{\rm iso}(n)\in\Psi_{0,K}\}
from the stopping event of
T^{*}_{fwer}
.
This finishes the first part of the proof. Next, we prove its asymptotic optimality.

First, let us fix any arbitrary
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
. In order to prove that this rule is asymptotically optimal under
{\sf P}
, it suffices to show that (
48
) holds when
s_{k}=\{k\}
for every
k\in[K]
. Then by Proposition
D.1
(iii), this will further imply that (
17
) in Theorem
4.4
is satisfied and the result will follow.
Fix some
\bar{k}\in[K]
such that

\bar{k}\in\argmin_{k\in[K]}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{G}^{k}),

and let
s=s_{\bar{k}}=\{\bar{k}\}
.
Note that, under
{\sf P}
,
s
is independent of
s^{c}
due to independence across the sources. Furthermore, for any
{\sf Q}_{1}
such that

{\sf Q}_{1}\in\argmin_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,\bar{k}}}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};\bar{k}\right)\quad\text{implies that}\quad{\sf Q}_{1}^{\bar{k}}\in\mathcal{G}^{\bar{k}}.

Thus,
|\mathcal{A}\left({\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\right)|=1
, and also, under
{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}
the sources are still independent, which imply that

{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}.

Therefore, (
48
) holds.

Next, we fix any arbitrary
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}
. In order to prove that this rule is asymptotically optimal under
{\sf P}
, we use Theorem
4.6
. We first show that (
47
) holds when
s^{\prime}_{k}=\{k\}
for every
k\in[K]
. Then by Proposition
D.1
(ii), this will further imply that (
16
) is satisfied.
Fix
\{\bar{k}\}\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
be the unit such that

\{\bar{k}\}\in\argmin_{\{k\}\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{G}^{k}),

and let
s=s_{\bar{k}}=\{\bar{k}\}
.
Note that, under
{\sf P}
,
s
is independent of
s^{c}
due to independence across the sources. Furthermore, for any
{\sf Q}_{1}
such that

{\sf Q}_{1}\in\argmin_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,\bar{k}}}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};\bar{k}\right)\quad\text{implies that}\quad{\sf Q}_{1}^{\bar{k}}\in\mathcal{G}^{\bar{k}}.

Thus,
|\mathcal{A}\left({\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\right)|=|\mathcal{A}({\sf P})|+1\geq 1
, and also, under
{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}
the sources are still independent, which imply that

{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}.

Therefore, (
47
) holds. Now, we also use Remark
4.4
to complete the rest of this proof.

First consider the case when
|\mathcal{A}({\sf P})|=1
and let
\mathcal{A}({\sf P})=\{\{k_{1}\}\}
. Since
s_{k}=s^{\prime}_{k}=\{k\}
for every
k\in[K]
,

\max_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0};s_{k})=\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k_{1}},\mathcal{H}^{k_{1}})=\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,k};s^{\prime}_{k}).

Furthermore, from Lemma
E.9
and Lemma
E.10
, we have

\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0})=\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k_{1}},\mathcal{H}^{k_{1}})\leq\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k_{1}},\mathcal{H}^{k_{1}})+\min_{k\neq k_{1}}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{G}^{k})=\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,k}).

Thus, (
19
) holds, and
also, the first equality in above implies that (
18
) is satisfied. Therefore, by using Remark
4.4
the result follows.

Next, we consider the case when
|\mathcal{A}({\sf P})|>1
. Since
s_{k}=s^{\prime}_{k}=\{k\}
for every
k\in[K]
,

\max_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0};s_{k})=\max_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{H}^{k})\geq\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{H}^{k})=\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,k};s^{\prime}_{k}).

Furthermore, from Lemma
E.9
and Lemma
E.10
, we have

\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0})=\sum_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{H}^{k})\geq\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{H}^{k})=\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,k}).

Thus, (
20
) holds, and also, the last equality in above implies that (
15
) is satisfied.
Therefore, by using Remark
4.4
the result follows.
This completes the proof.

(ii)

Since
s_{k}=\{k\}
,
s^{\prime}_{k}=[K]
, and
A_{k}=A
,
B_{k}=B
for every
k\in[K]
, we have
T^{*}_{fwer}=\min\{T_{0},T_{joint}\}
, where one can write the stopping times
T_{0}
and
T_{joint}
as

\displaystyle T_{0}

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\max_{k\in[K]}\;\Lambda_{k}(n)\leq 1/A\right\}=\inf\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\Lambda_{(1)}(n)\leq 1/A\right\},

\displaystyle T_{joint}

\displaystyle=\inf\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\;\max_{k\in[K]}\;\Lambda_{e}(n)\geq B,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\;\;\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)\notin\left(1/A,B\right)\;\;\text{for every}\,k\in[K]\quad\text{and}\quad 1\leq|D_{\rm iso}(n)|\leq u\}

\displaystyle=\inf\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\;\Lambda_{(1)}(n)\geq D,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\;\;\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)\notin\left(1/A,B\right)\;\;\text{for every}\,k\in[K]\quad\text{and}\quad 1\leq|D_{\rm iso}(n)|\leq u\}.

Since
\Lambda_{(1)}(n)\geq D>1
implies that
p(n)\geq 1
, the stopping events of
T_{joint}
remains the same if it is intersected with
p(n)\geq 1
. Again, from Lemmas
E.4
,
E.5
,
E.6
and
E.7
, one can observe that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and
u\leq K
,
p(n)\geq 1
implies
1\leq|D_{\rm iso}(n)|\leq u
.
Thus, one can safely replace the latter condition with the first and write
T_{joint}
as

\displaystyle T_{joint}

\displaystyle=\inf\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\;\Lambda_{(1)}(n)\geq D,

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\;\;\Lambda_{k,\rm iso}(n)\notin\left(1/A,B\right)\;\;\text{for every}\,k\in[K]\quad\text{and}\quad p(n)\geq 1\}.

Now, it is enough to show that
T_{joint}=\min\{T_{1},T_{2},T_{3},T_{4}\}
and in order to do so,
we further intersect the stopping event of
T_{joint}
with the disjoint decomposition of
\{p(n)\geq 1\}
into these following events:

\{p(n)\geq 1\}=\{p(n)=1\}\cup\{1<p(n)<u\}\cup\{p(n)=u\}\cup\{p(n)>u\}.

The rest of the proof for the first part can be done in a similar manner as employed in the proof of Theorem
5.3
. Next, we prove its asymptotic optimality.

First, let us fix any arbitrary
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
. In order to prove that this rule is asymptotically optimal under
{\sf P}
, it suffices to show that (
48
) holds when
s_{k}=\{k\}
for every
k\in[K]
. Then by Proposition
D.1
(iii), this will further imply that (
17
) in Theorem
4.4
is satisfied and the result will follow.
Fix some
\bar{k}\in[K]
such that

\bar{k}\in\argmin_{k\in[K]}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{G}^{k}),

and let
s=s_{\bar{k}}=\{\bar{k}\}
.
Note that, under
{\sf P}
,
s
is independent of
s^{c}
due to independence across the sources. Furthermore, for any
{\sf Q}_{1}
such that

{\sf Q}_{1}\in\argmin_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,\bar{k}}}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};\bar{k}\right)\quad\text{implies that}\quad{\sf Q}_{1}^{\bar{k}}\in\mathcal{G}^{\bar{k}}.

Thus,
|\mathcal{A}\left({\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\right)|=1
, and also, under
{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}
the sources are still independent, which imply that

{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}.

Therefore, (
48
) holds.

Next, we fix any arbitrary
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}
. In order to prove that this rule is asymptotically optimal under
{\sf P}
, we use Theorem
4.6
. Since
s^{\prime}_{k}=[K]
for every
k\in[K]
, both (
15
) and (
16
) are satisfied, however (
18
) is not satisfied when
|\mathcal{A}({\sf P})|>1
.
In this case, we use Remark
4.4
to solve this issue.

Since
|\mathcal{A}({\sf P})|>1
and
s_{k}=\{k\}
,
s^{\prime}_{k}=[K]
for every
k\in[K]
,

\displaystyle\max_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0};s_{k})

\displaystyle=\max_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{H}^{k})\geq\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{H}^{k})

\displaystyle=\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,k})=\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,k};s^{\prime}_{k}).

Furthermore, from Lemma
E.9
and Lemma
E.10
, we have

\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0})=\sum_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{H}^{k})\geq\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{H}^{k})=\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,k}).

Thus, (
20
) holds, and also, the last equality in above implies that (
15
) is satisfied.
Therefore, by using Remark
4.4
the result follows.
This completes the proof.

∎

F
Detection and isolation of anomalous sources: the general case

F.1
Proof of Theorem
5.5

We have
\emptyset\in\Psi
,
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}
and
s^{\prime}_{k}=\{k\}
for every
k\in[K]
.

(i)

If
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\to 0
such that
|\log\gamma|>>|\log\alpha|\vee|\log\delta|
, from Lemma
D.2
(iii) we have

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)\right\}\gtrsim\;\max_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\gamma|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,k})}\right\},

and on the other hand, from Theorem
4.2
(ii) we have

\displaystyle{\sf E}_{\sf P}\left[T^{*}\right]\lesssim\max_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\gamma|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{H}^{k}\right)}\right\}.

Thus, the result follows from the definition of
{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]
.

Now, note that, by definition
{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]\geq 1
. Therefore, to prove that
{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]=1
, it suffices to show that
(
15
) holds, i.e.,

\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{H}^{k})=\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,k}),

which again is true from Proposition
D.1
if (
46
) holds.
Fix the source
\bar{k}\in[K]
such that

\{\bar{k}\}\in\argmin_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{H}^{k}),

and for which
s
is independent of
s^{c}
under
{\sf P}
, where
s=s^{\prime}_{\bar{k}}=\{\bar{k}\}
. Furthermore, for any
{\sf Q}_{1}
such that

{\sf Q}_{1}\in\argmin_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,\bar{k}}}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};\bar{k}\right)\quad\text{implies that}\quad{\sf Q}_{1}^{\bar{k}}\in\mathcal{H}^{\bar{k}}.

Thus,
1\leq|\mathcal{A}\left({\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\right)|=|\mathcal{A}({\sf P})|-1\leq u-1
, which implies that

{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}.

Therefore, (
46
) holds and the result follows.

(ii)

If
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\to 0
such that
|\log\delta|>>|\log\alpha|\vee|\log\gamma|
, from Lemma
D.2
(iii) we have

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)\right\}\gtrsim\;\max_{\{k\}\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\delta|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,k})}\right\},

and on the other hand, from Theorem
4.2
(ii) we have

\displaystyle{\sf E}_{\sf P}\left[T^{*}\right]\lesssim\max_{\{k\}\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\delta|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{G}^{k}\right)}\right\}.

Thus, the result follows from the definition of
{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]
.

Now, note that, by definition
{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]\geq 1
. Therefore, to prove that
{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]=1
, it suffices to show that
(
16
) holds, i.e.,

\min_{\{k\}\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{G}^{k})=\min_{\{k\}\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,k}),

which again is true from Proposition
D.1
if (
47
) holds.
Fix the source
\bar{k}\in[K]
such that

\{\bar{k}\}\in\argmin_{\{k\}\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{G}^{k}),

and for which
s
is independent of
s^{c}
under
{\sf P}
, where
s=s^{\prime}_{\bar{k}}=\{\bar{k}\}
. Furthermore, for any
{\sf Q}_{1}
such that

{\sf Q}_{1}\in\argmin_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,\bar{k}}}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};\bar{k}\right)\quad\text{implies that}\quad{\sf Q}_{1}^{\bar{k}}\in\mathcal{G}^{\bar{k}}.

Thus,
2\leq|\mathcal{A}\left({\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\right)|=|\mathcal{A}({\sf P})|+1\leq u
, which implies that

{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}.

Therefore, (
47
) holds and the result follows.

∎

F.2
Proof of Theorem
5.6

We have
\emptyset\in\Psi
and
s_{k}=\{k\}
for every
k\in[K]
.

(i)

If
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}
and
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\to 0
such that
|\log\alpha|>>|\log\gamma|\vee|\log\delta|
, from Lemma
D.2
(iii) we have

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)\right\}\gtrsim\;\frac{|\log\alpha|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0})},

and on the other hand, from Theorem
4.2
(ii) we have

\displaystyle{\sf E}_{\sf P}\left[T^{*}\right]\lesssim\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\alpha|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{H}^{k}\right)}\right\}.

Thus, the result follows from the definition of
{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]
.

Now, note that, by definition
{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]\geq 1
. Therefore, to prove that
{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]=1
, it suffices to show that
(
18
) holds, i.e.,

\max_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{H}^{k})=\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0}),

which again is true from Proposition
D.1
if (
49
) holds.
Let the index of the only signal under
{\sf P}
be
\bar{k}
, i.e.,
\mathcal{A}({\sf P})=\{\{\bar{k}\}\}
. Then
s
is independent of
s^{c}
under
{\sf P}
, where
s=s_{\bar{k}}=\{\bar{k}\}
. Furthermore, for any
{\sf Q}_{1}
such that

{\sf Q}_{1}\in\argmin_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};\bar{k}\right)\quad\text{implies that}\quad{\sf Q}_{1}^{\bar{k}}\in\mathcal{H}^{\bar{k}}.

Thus,
|\mathcal{A}\left({\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\right)|=0
, which implies that

{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}.

Therefore, (
49
) holds and the result follows.

(ii)

If
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
and
\alpha,\beta\to 0
, while
\gamma
and
\delta
are either fixed or go to
0
, from Lemma
D.2
(i) we have

\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)\right\}\;\gtrsim\;\max_{k\in[K]}\left\{\frac{|\log\beta|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,k}\right)}\right\},

and on the other hand, from Theorem
4.2
(i) we have

{\sf E}_{\sf P}\left[T^{*}\right]\;\lesssim\;\max_{k\in[K]}\left\{\frac{|\log\beta|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{G}^{k}\right)}\right\}.

Thus, the result follows from the definition of
{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]
.

Now, note that, by definition
{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]\geq 1
. Therefore, to prove that
{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]=1
, it suffices to show that
(
17
) holds, i.e.,

\min_{k\in[K]}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{G}^{k})=\min_{k\in[K]}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,k}),

which again is true from Proposition
D.1
if (
48
) holds.
Fix the source
\bar{k}\in[K]
such that

\bar{k}\in\argmin_{k\in[K]}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{G}^{k}),

and for which
s
is independent of
s^{c}
under
{\sf P}
, where
s=s^{\prime}_{\bar{k}}=\{\bar{k}\}
. Furthermore, for any
{\sf Q}_{1}
such that

{\sf Q}_{1}\in\argmin_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,\bar{k}}}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};\bar{k}\right)\quad\text{implies that}\quad{\sf Q}_{1}^{\bar{k}}\in\mathcal{G}^{\bar{k}}.

Therefore,
|\mathcal{A}\left({\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\right)|=1
, and since
\Psi_{1,1}\subseteq\Psi
, this implies that

{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}.

Therefore, (
48
) holds and the result follows.

∎

F.3
Proof of Corollary
5.1

We have
\Psi=\Psi_{0,u}
and
s_{k}=s^{\prime}_{k}=\{k\}
for every
k\in[K]
. Fix any
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}
, and let
\mu_{\sf P}
be the mean vector and
\Sigma
be the covariance matrix of
X_{n}
under
{\sf P}
for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Then we have the decomposition that
\Sigma=URU
, where
U
is the diagonal matrix with elements of the main diagonal being the standard deviations of the sources, i.e.,

U={\rm{diag}}(\sigma_{1},\dots,\sigma_{K}).

Also, in the following we denote by
I_{K}
the
K\times K
identity matrix, and by
a_{k}
the column vector in
\mathbb{R}^{K}
with only the
k^{th}
element being
1
and the rest being
0
.

(i)

Fix any source
\bar{k}\in[K]
such that

\{\bar{k}\}\in\argmin_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}({\mu_{k}^{2}}/{\sigma_{k}^{2}}),

and consider the distribution
{\sf Q}
such that it differs from
{\sf P}
only in the mean of observations from the source
\bar{k}
, i.e., under
{\sf Q}
, we have
X^{\bar{k}}\overset{\text{iid}}{\sim}N(0,\sigma_{\bar{k}}^{2})
. To be more specific, if
\mu_{\sf Q}
denotes the mean vector of
X_{n}
under
{\sf Q}
, then it differs from
\mu_{\sf P}
only in its
\bar{k}^{th}
element, such that it is
\mu_{\bar{k}}
in
\mu_{\sf P}
but
0
in
\mu_{\sf Q}
. However, the covariance matrix of
X_{n}
is the same under both
{\sf P}
and
{\sf Q}
. Thus,
{\sf Q}^{\bar{k}}\in\mathcal{H}^{\bar{k}}
and
u\geq|\mathcal{A}({\sf Q})|=|\mathcal{A}({\sf P})|-1\geq 1
, which implies
{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,\bar{k}}
.
Now, from (
14
) we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\{\log\frac{det(\Sigma)}{det(\Sigma)}-K+tr(\Sigma^{-1}\Sigma)+(\mu_{\sf Q}-\mu_{\sf P})^{t}\Sigma^{-1}(\mu_{\sf Q}-\mu_{\sf P})\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\{-K+tr(I_{K})+(\mu_{\sf Q}-\mu_{\sf P})^{t}\Sigma^{-1}(\mu_{\sf Q}-\mu_{\sf P})\right\},

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu_{\bar{k}}^{2}}{2}a_{\bar{k}}^{t}U^{-1}R^{-1}U^{-1}a_{\bar{k}}=\frac{\mu_{\bar{k}}^{2}}{2\sigma_{\bar{k}}^{2}}a_{\bar{k}}^{t}R^{-1}a_{\bar{k}}=\frac{\mu_{\bar{k}}^{2}}{2\sigma_{\bar{k}}^{2}}R^{-1}_{\bar{k}\bar{k}}.

Therefore, as
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\to 0
such that
|\log\gamma|>>|\log\alpha|\vee|\log\delta|
, from Theorem
5.5
(i) we have

\displaystyle{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,k}\right)}{\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{H}^{k}\right)}=\frac{\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,k}\right)}{(1/2)\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}(\mu_{k}^{2}/\sigma_{k}^{2})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,\bar{k}}\right)}{\mu_{\bar{k}}^{2}/2\sigma_{\bar{k}}^{2}}\leq\frac{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q})}{\mu_{\bar{k}}^{2}/2\sigma_{\bar{k}}^{2}}=R^{-1}_{\bar{k}\bar{k}}.

Finally, the proof is complete by taking minimum over all such
\bar{k}
.

(ii)

Fix any source
\bar{k}\in[K]
such that

\{\bar{k}\}\in\argmin_{\{k\}\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\min_{\theta\in\mathcal{M}_{k}\setminus\{0\}}({\theta^{2}}/{\sigma_{k}^{2}}),

and consider the distribution
{\sf Q}
such that it differs from
{\sf P}
only in the mean of observations from the source
\bar{k}
, i.e., under
{\sf Q}
, we have
X^{\bar{k}}\overset{\text{iid}}{\sim}N(\theta_{\bar{k}},\sigma_{\bar{k}}^{2})
, where

\theta_{\bar{k}}:=\argmin_{\theta\in\mathcal{M}_{\bar{k}}\setminus\{0\}}({\theta^{2}}/{\sigma_{\bar{k}}^{2}}).

To be more specific, if
\mu_{\sf Q}
denotes the mean vector of
X_{n}
under
{\sf Q}
, then it differs from
\mu_{\sf P}
only in its
\bar{k}^{th}
element, such that it is
0
in
\mu_{\sf P}
but
\theta_{\bar{k}}
in
\mu_{\sf Q}
. However, the covariance matrix of
X_{n}
is the same under both
{\sf P}
and
{\sf Q}
. Thus,
{\sf Q}^{\bar{k}}\in\mathcal{G}^{\bar{k}}
and
u\geq|\mathcal{A}({\sf Q})|=|\mathcal{A}({\sf P})|+1\geq 1
, which implies
{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,\bar{k}}
.
Now, from (
14
) we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\{\log\frac{det(\Sigma)}{det(\Sigma)}-K+tr(\Sigma^{-1}\Sigma)+(\mu_{\sf Q}-\mu_{\sf P})^{t}\Sigma^{-1}(\mu_{\sf Q}-\mu_{\sf P})\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\{-K+tr(I_{K})+(\mu_{\sf Q}-\mu_{\sf P})^{t}\Sigma^{-1}(\mu_{\sf Q}-\mu_{\sf P})\right\},

\displaystyle=\frac{\theta_{\bar{k}}^{2}}{2}a_{\bar{k}}^{t}U^{-1}R^{-1}U^{-1}a_{\bar{k}}=\frac{\theta_{\bar{k}}^{2}}{2\sigma_{\bar{k}}^{2}}a_{\bar{k}}^{t}R^{-1}a_{\bar{k}}=\frac{\theta_{\bar{k}}^{2}}{2\sigma_{\bar{k}}^{2}}R^{-1}_{\bar{k}\bar{k}}.

Therefore, as
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\to 0
such that
|\log\delta|>>|\log\alpha|\vee|\log\gamma|
, from Theorem
5.5
(ii) we have

\displaystyle{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\min_{\{k\}\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,k}\right)}{\min_{\{k\}\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{G}^{k}\right)}=\frac{\min_{\{k\}\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,k}\right)}{(1/2)\min_{\{k\}\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\min_{\theta\in\mathcal{M}_{k}\setminus\{0\}}({\theta^{2}}/{\sigma_{k}^{2}})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,\bar{k}}\right)}{\theta_{\bar{k}}^{2}/2\sigma_{\bar{k}}^{2}}\leq\frac{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q})}{\theta_{\bar{k}}^{2}/2\sigma_{\bar{k}}^{2}}=R^{-1}_{\bar{k}\bar{k}}.

Finally, the proof is complete by taking minimum over all such
\bar{k}
.

(iii)

We make the following observation about
\mu_{\sf P}
which will be used in this proof:

\mu_{{\sf P}}=\sum_{\{i\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mu_{i}a_{i}.

Consider the distribution
{\sf Q}
such that the mean of observations from every source is
0
. To be more specific, if
\mu_{\sf Q}
denotes the mean vector of
X_{n}
under
{\sf Q}
, then every element of
\mu_{\sf Q}
is
0
. However, the covariance matrix of
X_{n}
is the same under both
{\sf P}
and
{\sf Q}
. Thus,
{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
.
Now, from (
14
) we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\{\log\frac{det(\Sigma)}{det(\Sigma)}-K+tr(\Sigma^{-1}\Sigma)+(\mu_{\sf Q}-\mu_{\sf P})^{t}\Sigma^{-1}(\mu_{\sf Q}-\mu_{\sf P})\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\{-K+tr(I_{K})+\mu_{\sf P}^{t}\Sigma^{-1}\mu_{\sf P}\right\},

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{\{i\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mu_{i}a_{i}\right)^{t}U^{-1}R^{-1}U^{-1}\left(\sum_{\{j\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mu_{j}a_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\{i\},\{j\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mu_{i}\mu_{j}a_{i}^{t}U^{-1}R^{-1}U^{-1}a_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\{i\},\{j\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\frac{\mu_{i}\mu_{j}}{\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}}a_{i}^{t}R^{-1}a_{j}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\{i\},\{j\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\frac{\mu_{i}\mu_{j}}{\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}}R^{-1}_{ij}

Therefore, as
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\to 0
such that
|\log\alpha|>>|\log\gamma|\vee|\log\delta|
, from Theorem
5.6
(i) we have

\displaystyle{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]

\displaystyle\leq\min_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0})}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{H}^{k})}\right\}\leq\frac{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q})}{(1/2)\max_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}(\mu_{k}^{2}/\sigma_{k}^{2})}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\{i\},\{j\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\frac{\mu_{i}R^{-1}_{ij}\mu_{j}}{\sigma_{i}\sigma_{j}}\bigg/\max_{\{k\}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\frac{\mu_{k}^{2}}{\sigma_{k}^{2}}.

(iv)

Since
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
all elements of
\mu_{\sf P}
are
0
.
Fix any source
\bar{k}\in[K]
such that

\bar{k}\in\argmin_{k\in[K]}\min_{\theta\in\mathcal{M}_{k}\setminus\{0\}}({\theta^{2}}/{\sigma_{k}^{2}}),

and consider the distribution
{\sf Q}
such that it differs from
{\sf P}
only in the mean of observations from the source
\bar{k}
, i.e., under
{\sf Q}
, we have
X^{\bar{k}}\overset{\text{iid}}{\sim}N(\theta_{\bar{k}},\sigma_{\bar{k}}^{2})
, where

\theta_{\bar{k}}:=\argmin_{\theta\in\mathcal{M}_{\bar{k}}\setminus\{0\}}({\theta^{2}}/{\sigma_{\bar{k}}^{2}}).

To be more specific, if
\mu_{\sf Q}
denotes the mean vector of
X_{n}
under
{\sf Q}
, then its
\bar{k}^{th}
element is
\theta_{\bar{k}}
and the rest are
0
, or
\mu_{\sf Q}=\theta_{\bar{k}}a_{\bar{k}}
. However, the covariance matrix of
X_{n}
is the same under both
{\sf P}
and
{\sf Q}
. Thus,
{\sf Q}^{\bar{k}}\in\mathcal{G}^{\bar{k}}
and
|\mathcal{A}({\sf Q})|=1
, which implies
{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,\bar{k}}
.
Now, from (
14
) we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\{\log\frac{det(\Sigma)}{det(\Sigma)}-K+tr(\Sigma^{-1}\Sigma)+(\mu_{\sf Q}-\mu_{\sf P})^{t}\Sigma^{-1}(\mu_{\sf Q}-\mu_{\sf P})\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\{-K+tr(I_{K})+\mu_{\sf Q}^{t}\Sigma^{-1}\mu_{\sf Q}\right\},

\displaystyle=\frac{\theta_{\bar{k}}^{2}}{2}a_{\bar{k}}^{t}U^{-1}R^{-1}U^{-1}a_{\bar{k}}=\frac{\theta_{\bar{k}}^{2}}{2\sigma_{\bar{k}}^{2}}a_{\bar{k}}^{t}R^{-1}a_{\bar{k}}=\frac{\theta_{\bar{k}}^{2}}{2\sigma_{\bar{k}}^{2}}R^{-1}_{\bar{k}\bar{k}}.

Therefore, as
\alpha,\beta\to 0
, while
\gamma
and
\delta
are either fixed or go to
0
, from Theorem
5.6
(ii) we have

\displaystyle{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\min_{k\in[K]}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,k}\right)}{\min_{k\in[K]}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P}^{k},\mathcal{G}^{k}\right)}=\frac{\min_{k\in[K]}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,k}\right)}{(1/2)\min_{k\in[K]}\min_{\theta\in\mathcal{M}_{k}\setminus\{0\}}({\theta^{2}}/{\sigma_{k}^{2}})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,\bar{k}}\right)}{\theta_{\bar{k}}^{2}/2\sigma_{\bar{k}}^{2}}\leq\frac{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},{\sf Q})}{\theta_{\bar{k}}^{2}/2\sigma_{\bar{k}}^{2}}=R^{-1}_{\bar{k}\bar{k}}.

Finally, the proof is complete by taking minimum over all such
\bar{k}
.

∎

G
Detection and isolation of a dependence structure

G.1
Proof of Theorem
6.1

Fix any arbitrary reference distribution
{\sf P}_{0}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
. For every
e\in\mathcal{K}
define

\Lambda^{1}_{e}(n):=\max\{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf P}_{0}^{e}):{\sf P}\in\mathcal{G}^{e}\}\quad\text{and}\quad\Lambda^{0}_{e}(n):=\max\{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf P}_{0}^{e}):{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}^{e}\}.

Then, for every
e\in\mathcal{K}
,

\Lambda_{e}(n)=\Lambda^{1}_{e}(n)/\Lambda^{0}_{e}(n).

(59)

Furthermore, since the dependent pairs are disjoint and (
23
) holds, we can write

{\sf P}=\bigotimes_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}{\sf P}^{e}\otimes{\sf P}^{v_{0}},\quad\text{and}\quad{\sf P}_{0}=\bigotimes_{k\in[K]}{\sf P}^{k}=\bigotimes_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}{\sf P}_{0}^{e}\otimes{\sf P}_{0}^{v_{0}},

where
v_{0}
is the only subset of
[K]
that consists of the independent sources under
{\sf P}
.
This implies

\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf P}_{0})=\prod_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\Lambda_{n}({\sf P}^{e},{\sf P}_{0}^{e})\times\Lambda_{n}({\sf P}^{v_{0}},{\sf P}_{0}^{v_{0}}).

(60)

Now, fix any arbitrary
\bar{e}\in\mathcal{K}
and a subset of units
A\in\Psi
such that
\bar{e}\in A
. Then under any
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,\bar{e}}
such that
\mathcal{A}({\sf P})=A
, the subset of independent sources is

v_{0}=[K]\setminus\cup_{e\in A}e.

Thus, from (
60
) one can obtain that

\displaystyle\max\{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf P}_{0}):{\sf P}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,\bar{e}},\;\;\mathcal{A}({\sf P})=A\}=\prod_{e\in A}\Lambda^{1}_{e}(n)\times\max\{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf P}_{0}^{v_{0}}):{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}^{v_{0}}\},\;\;\text{and}

\displaystyle\max\{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf P}_{0}):{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}\}=\prod_{e\in A}\Lambda_{e}^{0}(n)\times\max\{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf P}_{0}^{v_{0}}):{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}^{v_{0}}\}.

Due to (
59
), the above two expressions lead to

\frac{\max\{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf P}_{0}):{\sf P}\in\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,\bar{e}},\;\;\mathcal{A}({\sf P})=A\}}{\max\{\Lambda_{n}({\sf P},{\sf P}_{0}):{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}\}}=\prod_{e\in A}\Lambda_{e}(n),

and thus, if we further maximize the above quantity over all possible
A\in\Psi
, we have

\Lambda_{\bar{e},\rm det}(n)=\max_{A\in\Psi}\prod_{e\in A}\Lambda_{e}(n)=\Lambda_{\bar{e}}(n)\times\max_{\mathcal{B}\subseteq\{i_{1}(n),\dots i_{1\vee p(n)}(n)\},\;\bar{e}\cup\mathcal{B}\in\Psi}\prod_{e\in\mathcal{B}}\Lambda_{e}(n).

(61)

When
p(n)\geq 1
, using (
61
) and that
\Lambda_{\bar{e}}(n)\leq 1
for any
\bar{e}\notin\{i_{1}(n),\dots i_{p(n)}(n)\}
, we have

\max_{\bar{e}\in\mathcal{K}}\Lambda_{\bar{e},\rm det}(n)=\max_{\mathcal{B}\subseteq\{i_{1}(n),\dots i_{p(n)}(n)\},\;\mathcal{B}\in\Psi}\prod_{e\in\mathcal{B}}\Lambda_{e}(n).

Furthermore, again from (
61
) one can observe that

\max_{\bar{e}\in\mathcal{K}}\Lambda_{\bar{e},\rm det}(n)=\max_{\bar{e}\in\mathcal{K}}\Lambda_{\bar{e}}(n)=\Lambda_{(1)}(n)<1\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad p(n)=0.

Now, since
C_{e}=C
and
D_{e}=D
for every
e\in\mathcal{K}
, one can write the stopping times
T_{0}
and
T_{det}
as

\displaystyle T_{0}

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\max_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\;\Lambda_{e,\rm det}(n)\leq 1/C\right\},

\displaystyle T_{det}

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\max_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\;\Lambda_{e,\rm det}(n)\geq D\right\}.

Since
C,D>1
, stopping happens by
T_{0}
(resp.
T_{det}
) only if
\max_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\;\Lambda_{e,\rm det}(n)\leq 1
(resp.
\max_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\;\Lambda_{e,\rm det}(n)>1
), and from the first part of the proof, this happens only if
p(n)=0
(resp.
p(n)\geq 1
). Therefore, the stopping events in
T_{0}
and
T_{det}
remain the same even if they are intersected with
p(n)=0
and
p(n)\geq 1
respectively.
Thus,

\displaystyle T_{0}

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\max_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\;\Lambda_{e,\rm det}(n)\leq 1/C,\;\;p(n)=0\right\}

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\Lambda_{(1)}(n)\leq 1/C\right\},

and

\displaystyle T_{det}

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\max_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\;\Lambda_{e,\rm det}(n)\geq D,\;\;p(n)\geq 1\right\}

\displaystyle=\inf\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}:\max_{\mathcal{B}\subseteq\{i_{1}(n),\dots i_{p(n)}(n)\},\;\mathcal{B}\in\Psi}\prod_{e\in\mathcal{B}}\Lambda_{e}(n)\geq D\right\},

This completes the proof.
∎

G.2
An important lemma

We present an important lemma which will be useful to establish the following theorems.

Lemma G.1
.

Suppose that
\emptyset\in\Psi
and
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}
.

(i)

If
{\sf P}\notin\mathcal{H}_{0}
and for every
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
there exists
w_{e}\supseteq e
such that (
15
) holds when
s^{\prime}_{e}=w_{e}
for every
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
, i.e.,

\min_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e};w_{e}\right)=\min_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}\right),

then it also holds when
s^{\prime}_{e}\supseteq w_{e}
for every
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
.

(ii)

If
{\sf P}\notin\mathcal{H}_{0}
and for every
e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
there exists
w_{e}\supseteq e
such that (
16
) holds when
s^{\prime}_{e}=w_{e}
for every
e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
, i.e.,

\min_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e};w_{e}\right)=\min_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}\right),

then it also holds when
s^{\prime}_{e}\supseteq w_{e}
for every
e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
.

(iii)

If
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{H}_{0}
and for every
e\in\mathcal{K}
there exists
w_{e}\supseteq e
such that (
17
) holds when
s_{e}=w_{e}
for every
e\in\mathcal{K}
, i.e.,

\min_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\;\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e};w_{e}\right)=\min_{e\in\mathcal{K}}\;\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}\right),

then it also holds when
s_{e}\supseteq w_{e}
for every
e\in\mathcal{K}
.

(iv)

If
{\sf P}\notin\mathcal{H}_{0}
and for some
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
there exists
w_{e}\supseteq e
such that

\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0};w_{e})=\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0}),

then (
18
) holds when
s_{e}\supseteq w_{e}
.

We only prove (i) and (iv) since the other results can be shown in a similar way. Let
s^{\prime}_{e}\supseteq w_{e}
for every
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
. Then,

\displaystyle\min_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\min_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e};s^{\prime}_{e}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\min_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e};w_{e}\right)=\min_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}\right),

where the first and the second inequality follow by using Lemma
B.2
and the fact that
[K]\supseteq s^{\prime}_{e}\supseteq w_{e}
for every
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
. Thus, equality holds throughout the above expression and (
15
) is implied.

In order to prove (iv), let
s_{\bar{e}}\supseteq w_{\bar{e}}
for some
\bar{e}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
such that

\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0};w_{\bar{e}})=\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0}).

Then,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0})

\displaystyle\geq\max_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0};s_{e})

\displaystyle\geq\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0};s_{\bar{e}})\geq\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0};w_{\bar{e}})=\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{0}),

where the first and third inequality follow by using Lemma
B.2
and the facts that
[K]\supseteq s_{e}
for every
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
as well as
s_{\bar{e}}\supseteq w_{\bar{e}}
.
∎

G.3
Proof of Theorem
6.2

We have
\emptyset\in\Psi
,
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}
, and
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\to 0
such that
|\log\gamma|>>|\log\alpha|\vee|\log\delta|
.

(i)

From Lemma
D.2
(iii) we have

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)\right\}\gtrsim\;\max_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\gamma|}{\mathcal{I}({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e})}\right\},

and on the other hand, from Theorem
4.2
(ii) we have

\displaystyle{\sf E}_{\sf P}\left[T^{*}\right]\lesssim\max_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\gamma|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e};s^{\prime}_{e}\right)}\right\}.

Thus, from definition we have

{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]\leq\frac{\min_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}\right)}{\min_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e};s^{\prime}_{e}\right)}.

Since by definition
{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]\geq 1
, to prove that
{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]=1
, it suffices to show that
(
15
) holds, i.e.,

\min_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e};s^{\prime}_{e}\right)=\min_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}\right).

In view of Lemma
G.1
(i), the above is true if (
46
) in Proposition
D.1
holds when for every
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})

s^{\prime}_{e}=v_{l}\;\;\text{where}\;\;v_{l}\supseteq e\;\;\text{for some}\;\;l\in[L({\sf P})].

Fix any arbitrary
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
.
Then for every
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
we have
(s^{\prime}_{e})^{c}=\cup_{k\neq l}v_{k}
which is independent of
v_{l}
, that is,
s^{\prime}_{e}
. Furthermore, letting

{\sf Q}_{1}=\argmin_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};s^{\prime}_{e}\right)\quad\text{implies that}\quad{\sf Q}_{1}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}=\argmin_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}^{s^{\prime}_{e}}}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{s^{\prime}_{e}},{\sf Q}).

Note that, since
L({\sf P})>1
, there exists
l^{\prime}\neq l
,
l^{\prime}\geq 1
and
e^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
such that
e^{\prime}\subseteq v_{l^{\prime}}\subseteq(s^{\prime}_{e})^{c}
. Therefore, denoting
s=s^{\prime}_{e}
, we have
e^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}\left({\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\right)\in\Psi
. Thus,

{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0},

which implies that (
46
) holds and the result follows.

(ii)

If
s^{\prime}_{e}=e
for every
e\in\mathcal{K}
then the upper bound on
{\sf{ARE}}
follows from part (i).

•

Since
\Psi=\Psi_{dis}
we have
L({\sf P})=|\mathcal{A}({\sf P})|>1
. Furthermore, due to (
23
) for every
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})

there exists
l\in[L({\sf P})]
such that
v_{l}=e=s^{\prime}_{e}
. Therefore, the condition in part (i) is satisfied and the result follows.

•

It suffices to show that (
15
) holds, i.e.,

\min_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P}^{e},\mathcal{H}^{e}\right)=\min_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,e}\right),

which again is true from Proposition
D.1
if (
46
) holds.
Fix the pair
\bar{e}\in\mathcal{K}
such that

\bar{e}\in\argmin_{e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}({\sf P}^{e},\mathcal{H}^{e}),

and for which
s
is independent of
s^{c}
under
{\sf P}
, where
s=s^{\prime}_{\bar{e}}=\bar{e}
. Furthermore, for any
{\sf Q}_{1}
such that

{\sf Q}_{1}\in\argmin_{{\sf Q}\in\mathcal{H}_{\Psi,\bar{e}}}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},{\sf Q};\bar{e}\right)\quad\text{implies that}\quad{\sf Q}_{1}^{\bar{e}}\in\mathcal{H}^{\bar{e}}.

Since
s
is independent of
s^{c}
, any
e\in\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
such that
e\neq\bar{e}
must satisfy
e\subseteq s^{c}
, and the number of such pairs is greater than or equal to
1
as
L({\sf P})>1
. Combined with the above, the fact that
\mathcal{A}({\sf P})\in\Psi
leads to
\emptyset\neq\mathcal{A}\left({\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\right)\in\Psi
when
\Psi
is either
\Psi_{dis}
, or
\Psi_{clus}
, or the powerset of
\mathcal{K}
. Thus,

{\sf Q}_{1}^{s}\otimes{\sf P}^{s^{c}}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0},

which implies that (
46
) holds and the result follows.

∎

G.4
Proof of Theorem
6.3

We have
\emptyset\in\Psi
,
{\sf P}\in\mathcal{P}_{\Psi}\setminus\mathcal{H}_{0}
, and
\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\to 0
such that
|\log\delta|>>|\log\alpha|\vee|\log\gamma|
.

(i)

From Lemma
D.2
(iii) we have

\displaystyle\inf\left\{{\sf E}_{{\sf P}}[T]:\;(T,D)\in\mathcal{C}_{\Psi}(\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta)\right\}\gtrsim\;\max_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\delta|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}\right)}\right\},

and on the other hand, from Theorem
4.2
(ii) we have

\displaystyle{\sf E}_{\sf P}\left[T^{*}\right]\lesssim\max_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\left\{\frac{|\log\delta|}{\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e};s^{\prime}_{e}\right)}\right\}.

Thus, from definition we have

{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]\leq\frac{\min_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}\right)}{\min_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e};s^{\prime}_{e}\right)}.

Since by definition
{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]\geq 1
, to prove that
{\sf{ARE}}_{{\sf P}}[\chi^{*}]=1
, it suffices to show that
(
16
) holds, i.e.,

\min_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e};s^{\prime}_{e}\right)=\min_{e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})}\mathcal{I}\left({\sf P},\mathcal{G}_{\Psi,e}\right).

In view of Lemma
G.1
(ii), the above is true if (
47
) in Proposition
D.1
holds when for every
e=\{i,j\}\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
we have

s^{\prime}_{e}=\begin{cases}v_{l}\quad&\text{if}\quad v_{l}\supseteq e\quad\text{for some}\quad l\in[L({\sf P})],\\
v_{l}\cup v_{l^{\prime}}\quad&\text{if}\quad i\in v_{l}\quad\text{and}\quad j\in v_{l^{\prime}}\quad\text{for some}\quad l\neq l^{\prime},\quad l,l^{\prime}\in[L({\sf P})],\\
e\cup v_{l}\quad&\text{if}\quad i\;\;\text{or}\;\;j\in v_{0}\quad\text{and}\quad e\setminus v_{0}\in v_{l}\quad\text{for some}\quad l\in[L({\sf P})],\\
e\quad&\text{if}\quad v_{0}\supseteq e.\end{cases}

Fix any arbitrary
e\notin\mathcal{A}({\sf P})
.
Then in the first case, we have
(s^{\prime}_{e})^{c}=\cup_{k\neq l}v_{k}
which is independent of
v_{l}
. In the second case, we have
(s^{\prime}_{e})^{c}=\cup_{k\neq l,l^{\prime}}v_{k}
which is independent of both
v_{l}
and
v_{l^{\prime}}
. In the third case, we have
